     611.1.       Purpose and Severability of Ordinance.
          .1-1. PURPOSE OF ORDINANCE.
          •1-2. SEVERABILITY OF ORDINANCE.
     .1-1. PURPOSE OF ORDINANCE.
          This ordinance is enacted to preserve and promote the public heal~, safety
     and weliare of the inhabitants of the City and County of Denver, and of the
     public generally, and to encourage and facilitate the orderly growth and expan•
     sion of the municipality.
     •1-2. SEVERABILITY OF ORDINANCE.                                                      .'
          If for any :reason any one or more sections, sentences, clauses or parts of
     this ordinance are held invalid, such judgment shall not affect, impair or invalid-
     ate the remaining provisions of this ordinance hut shall he confined in its
     operation to the specific sections, sentences, clauses or parts of this ordinance
     held invalid and the invalidity of any section, sentence, clause or part of this
     ordinance in any one or more instances shall not affect or prejudice in any
     way the validity of this ordinance in any other instance.

     611.2.       Prohibitions, Violations and Remedies .

    .2-1.    PROHIBITIONS •

    .2-1 (1).     LIMITATIONS ON ALL LAND AND STRUCTURES.
            No land shall be used or occupied and no structure shall be designed,
    erected, altered, used or occupied except in conformity with all regulations
    herein established and upon pedormance of all conditions herein set forth.
    .2-1 (2). LIMITATIONS ON SALES AND RENTALS OF ALL LAND AND STRUCTURES.
         No person, firm or corporation and no officer or employee thereof (either
    as owner or as participating principal, agent, servant or employee of such
    owner) shall sell, rent or lease or offer or attempt to sell, rent or lease any
    land or structure upon the representation, falsely made and known to be false,
    that such land or structure may he used or occupied in a manner or for a use
    prohibited by this ordinance.
    .2-1 (3). LIMITATIONS ON MUNICIPAL AGENCIES.
          No permit, certificate, license or other document or oral approval, the use
    of which may be subject to the provisions of this ordinance, shall be issued by
    any department, agency or board of the municipality until the Department of
    Zoning Administration shall have certified that the use to be made of the permit,
    certificate, license, other document or oral approval is in full compliance with
    the provisions of this ordinance.
    .2-1 (4). ZONING PERMIT TO ERECT OR ALTER STRUCTURES.
         No structure shall be erected or altered until a Zoning Permit for such
    erection or alteration shall have been issued by the Department of Zoning
    Administration.
    .2-1 (5). ZoNING PERMIT TO UsE oR OccuPY SmucTURES.
          No structure shall be used or occupied until a Zoning Permit for such use
    and occupancy shall have been issued by the Department of Zoning Admin-
    istration .
    .2-1 ( 6). ZONING PERMIT FOR CHANGE OF USE.
         Neither the use of or the uses upon any land nor the use of or the uses
    within any structure shall be changed until a Zoning Permit for such change
    of use shall have been issued by the Department of Zoning Administration .
    .2-2.    VIOLATIONS •

    •2-2. (1). ESTABLISHED.
          Wherever by the provisions of this ordinance the pedormance of any act
     is required or the performance of any act is prohibited or wherever any regula-
    tion, dimension or limitation is imposed on the use or change of use of or upon
    any land or on the erection or alteration of any structure or the use or change
     of use of such structure or the uses within such structure, a failure to comply
    with the provisions of this ordinance shall constitute a violation of this ordi-
    nance. Every day on which a violation exists shall constitute a separate violation
    and a separate offense.
    .2-3.     REMEDIES .
    .2-3 (1). PENALTIES.
         Any person, firm or corporation violating any provision of this ordinance
.   shall be subject to the penalties provided by ordinance or by the Charter of the
    City and County of Denver.

            .2=3 (2). ADDITIONAL REMEDIES.
                  In case any building or structure is erected, constructed, reconstructed,
            altered, repaired, converted or maintained or any building, structure or land
            is used in violation of this ordinance, the municipality or any proper person
            may institute any appropriate action or proceedings to prevent such unlawful
            erection, construction, reconstruction, alteration, repair, conversion, mainte-
            nance or use, to restrain, correct or abate such violation, to prevent the occu-
            pancy of said building, structure or land or to prevent any illegal act, conduct,
            business or use in or about such premises. The imposition of any penalty
            hereunder shall not preclude the municipality or any proper person from insti-
            tuting any appropriate action or proceedings to require compliance with the
            provisions of this ordinance and with administrative orders and determinations
            made hereunder.

.4-1. RULES OF CONSTRUCTION .
.4-1 (I).
    Words, phrases and terms defined herein shall be given the defined meaning .
.4-1 (2).
     Words, phrases and terms not defined herein but defined in the Building
Code of the municipality shall be construed as defined in such Code .
.4-1 (3).
     Words, phrases and terms neither defined herein nor in the Building Code
of the municipality shall be given their usual and customary meanings except
where the context clearly indicates a different meaning .
.4-1 (4).
     The text of the ordinance shall control captions, titles and maps .
.4-1 (5).
     Where any requirement of this ordinance results in a fraction of a unit,
a fraction of five-tenths or more shall be considered a whole unit and a fraction
of less than five-tenths shall be disregarded .
.4-1 (6).
     The word "shall" is mandatory and not permissive; the word "may" is
permissive and not mandatory .
.4-1 (7).
     Words used in the singular include the plural and words used in the plural
include the singular .
.4-1 (8).
     Words used in the present tense include the future tense and words used
in the future tense include the present tense .
.4-2. TERMINATION OF AUTHORIZATIONS TO ERECT, ALTER,
      OCCUPY OR USE LAND OR STRUCTURES. EXCEPTION.
     Every authorization and permission, of whatever kind and nature and
however obtained, heretofore granted by the municipality or any department,
board, agency, officer or employee thereof to use or occupy any land or to design,
erect, alter , use or occupy any structure in any manner other than in full com-
pliance with all provisions of this ordinance shall terminate and expire on
the effective date of th is ordinance• and shall not thereafter be renewed or
revived. Nothing herein contained shall require any change in the plans, con-
struction or designated use of a building for which a building permit has been
heretofore legally issued and the construction of which building shall have
actually begun within ninety days of the date of such permit and the ground

=== page 6 ===
53   42




          story framework of which building, including the second tier of beams, shall
          ha ve been completed within one year of the date of such ·p ermit and which
          entire building shall be completed, according to such plans ·as are filed, withia
          two years from the effective date of this ordinance; provided, however, that any
          permit which does not authorize the alteration or erection of a designated
          building on the basis of complete plans and specifications shall not be deemed
          a building permit and shall not come within the foregoing exclusion .
          .4-3.  PENDING ACTIONS AND PROCEEDINGS.
               All actions and proceedings pending in any court of record and in the
          Supreme Court of the State of Colorado on the effective date of this ordinance
          shall be treated until the conclusion of such actions and proceedings as though
          all prior applicable zoning ordinances and amendments thereto were in full
          force and effect. All other actions and proceedings, of every kind and nature
          and whether judicial or administrative, pending on the effective date of this
          ordinance shall be governed by the provisions of this ordinance .
          .4-4.  OFFENSES AND LIABILITIES PRESERVED.
               All offenses committed and all liabilities incurred prior to the effective
          date of this ordinance shall be treated as though all prior applicable zoning
          ordinances and amendments thereto were in full force and effect for the purpose
          of sustaining any proper suit, action or prosecution with respect to such offenses
          and liabilities.
          .4-5.  EFFECT OF OTHER ORDINANCES AND REGULATIONS.
               Wherever higher or more restrictive standards are established by the pro-
          visions of any other applicable statute, ordinance or regulation than are estab-
          lished by the provisions of this ordinance. the provisions of such other statute,
          ordinance or regulation shall govern .
          .4-6.   EFFECT OF PRIVATE COVENANTS.
               Nothing herein contained shall be construed to render inoperative any
          restrictions established by covenants running with the land unless such restric-
          tions are prohibited by or are contrary to the provisions of this ordinance .
          .4-7.
              DISTRICT REGULATIONS TO BE UNIFORM. EXCEPTION.
           The regulations herein established shall apply uniformly to all geographical
      areas having the same district classification and bearing the same symbol or
      designation on the official map; provided, however, that if a district boundary
      line established by this ordinance, or by an amendment hereto, divides an area
      of land thereafter designated as a single Zone Lot pursuant to and in accordance
      with the provisions of this ordinance and if the entire area of land included
      within the designated Zone Lot was held in one ownership of record on the date
      the district boundary line was established, then and in that event :
      .4-7 (1).
            Each parcel of land of the area so divided may be used in conformity
       ..;th and subject to the regulations herein established for the district in which
       eac uch parcel of land is located; or
       4 7 (2) .
                If tJ_ie area i.s divided into two or more parcels of land, the entire area may
          be used m conforn,ity with and subject to the regulations herein established for
          the. district i_n which is located the parcel of land, if any such parcel there be,
          which con tams more than one-half of the area of the entire Zone Lot; or

.4-7 (3).
     If the area is divided into two equal partels of land, the entire area may
be used in conformity with and subject to the regulations herein established
for the district in which either of such parcels of land is located ; provided,
however,
.4-7 (4).
     That no parcel of land containing more than 6,250 square feet shall be
used in any manner except in conformity with and subject to the regulations
herein established for the district in which such parcel of land is located.

.4-8. LOCATING DISTRICT BOUNDARY LINES NOT READILY
       DETERMINABLE FROM OFFICIAL MAP.
     If for any reason the location of any district boundary line is not readily
determinable from the official map, the location of the district boundary line
shall be fixed by the Department of Zoning Administration in accordance with
the following provisions. Where more than one of the following provisions are
applicable in any given situation, the first enumerated applicable provision
shall prevail over all other provisions:
.4-8 (1).
     Where a district boundary line is located with reference to a fixture or
monument, the location of such fixture or monument shall control ;
.4-8 (2).
     Where a district boundary line is given a position within a street or alley
right-of-way or channelized waterway, the district boundary line shall be deemed
to be in the center of such street or alley right-of-way or channdized waterway;
.4-8 (3).
     Where a district boundary line is shown as approximately following platted
lot lines, the district boundary line shall be deemed to coincide with such
platted lot lines;
 .4-8 (4).
      Where a district boundary line is shown by a specific dimension, such
.specific dimension shall control ;
.4-8 (5).
     Where a district boundary line extends in the direction of the length of a
block, the district boundary line shall be deemed to coincide with the center
line of such block;
.4-8 (6).
     Where a district boundary line divides a platted lot or crosses unsubdivided
property, the location of the district boundary line shall be fixed from the
scale of the official map .

.4-9. STATUTE OF LIMITATIONS FOR MUNICIPALITY ONLY.
     All actions by the municipality to restrain, correct or abate the unlawful
location, bulk or gross floor area of or in a structure and alleted. to result from
the unlawful issuance of a permit to erect or alter such lllslidare, snail be
brought within three years after the issuance of the partieliUt permit alleged
to have been unlawfully issued and not after that period.

          .4-10. ZONE LOT FOR EXISTING STRUCTURES AND USES.
               The land area occupied by a use and/ or the building site heretofore desig-
          nated and occupied by each structure existing on the effective date of this
          ordinance (February 11, 1955) shall, for the purposes of this ordinance, be
          deemed the Zone Lot for such use and/ or structure and shall be continuously
          provided and maintained therefor in accordance with the provisions of this
          ordinance for uses and/ or structures in the district in which each such use
          and/ or structure is located. Upon application to and approval by the Depart-
          ment of Zoning Administration, the boundaries and area of a designated Zone
          Lot may be amended if full compliance with all requirements of this ordinance
          can be maintained.

          .5-1.  CREATION.
                There shall be and hereby is created a Department of Zoning Administration
          ( hereinafter called the "Department" l.
          .5-2. MANAGEMENT AND STAFF.                                                         "
               The Zoning Administrator shall be the officer in full charge of the Depart-
          ment and shall devote his entire time to the duties of his office. He shall be
          appointed by the Mayor and shall hold his office at the pleasure of the Mayor.
          The salary of the Zoning Administrator, his staff of assistants and the salaries
          of such assistants shall be fixed by Council. No member of the staff of the
          Department, including the Zoning Administrator, shall be employed by or be
          a member of the Board of Adjustment .
          . 5-3. DUTIES AND POWERS .
          .5-3 (1). ADMINISTRATION.
                There is hereby vested in the Department the duty of administering this
          ordinance and the power necessary for such administration, incidental to which
          duty and power the Department shall:
                .5-3 (1) {a). Intervene, for and on behalf of the municipality, in all
          public hearings before the Board of Adjustment, present facts and information
          to assist the Board in reaching a decision, resist and oppose any deviations from
          the standard provisions of this ordinance and have decisions of the Board
          reviewed in a court of proper jurisdiction when, in the judgment of the Zoning
          Administrator and with the approval of the Mayor, such review is desirable;
               .5-3 ( 1) ( b) . Propose and recommend to Council the enactment of amend-
          ments to this ordinance for the purpose of improving administration and
          enforcement of this ordinance;

and power the Department shall:

ance or non-compliance with the provisions of this ordinance. Incidental to
such investigations and surveys, an authorized representative of the Department
may enter into and upon and cause any land or structure to be inspected and
examined. A failure or a refusal to permit such entry and inspection, after the
issuance by the Department of an order therefor, shall constitute a violation
of this ordinance. Additionally, the right to entry and inspection may he en-
forced by application to and proper orders from a court of proper jurisdiction;

          .5-4.    APPEALS FROM THE DEPARTMENT.
          .5-4 ( 1 ) . PROCEDURE .
                Any person aggrieved or any officer or departme nt of the municipality
          may appeal to the Board of Adjustment from an y order or decis ion of the
          Department. Such appeal shall be taken by filing with the Department and with
          the Board of Adjustment, within the time provided by the rules of the Board,
          a noti ce of appeal specif ying the particular grounds upon whi ch the appeal
          is taken. Upon receipt of a notice of appeal, the Department shall transmit to
          the Board of Adjustment all of the original documents and materials, or true
          copies thereof. constituting the record upon whi ch the order or decision appealed
          from was based .
          .5-4 ( 2). EFFECT OF APPEAL.
                An appeal from the Department to the Board of Adj ustmcnt shall stay all
          proceedings unless the Zoning Administrator certifies that, by reason of the
          fact s stated in the certificate, a stay in his opinion wo uld cause imminent peril
          to life or property. When such a certificate is filed. proceedings shall n ot be
          stayed except by a restraining order granted, after due notice to the Department,
          by the Board of Adjustment or a court of proper jurisdicti on.

 .6-1.  CREATION.
      There shall be and hereby is created a Board of «-dJustment (hermnafter
called the "Board") consisting of five members. The members of the Board
 shall be appointed by Council for a term of five years and may be removed only
for cause upon written charges and after public hearing. The members of the
Board serving on the effective date of this ordinance, under a zoning ordinance
effective prior hereto, shall be and constitute the first Board hereunder and each
member thereof shall serve the balance of the term to which he was appointed.
Any vacancy which occurs in the Board shall be filled by Council for the unex•
pired term of any member whose term became vacant. The compensation of
the members of the Board shall be fixed by Council No member of the Board
shall he on the staff of the Board or be employed in or by the Department of
Zoning Administration.
.6-2.   STAFF.
      The staff of the Board shall consist of a Technical-Secretary and such other
assistants as may be authorized by Council. The Technical-Secretary shall be
the technical advisor to the Board and custodian of its records, shall conduct
official correspondence and generally supervise the clerical and technical work
of the Board. He shall be appointed by the Board and shall devote his entire
time to the duties of his office. The salary of the Technical-Secretary, the
number of additional assistants and the salaries of such additional assistants
shall be fixed by Council. No member of the staff shall be a member of the
Board or be employed in or by the Department of Zoning Administration.
•6-3.  RULES FOR PROCEEDINGS BEFORE BOARD.
     The Board shall adopt rules governing all proceedings before it. Such rules
shall provide and require that:
     .6-3 ( 1) . Public notice shall be given of all hearings and all hearings
shall be open to the public;
     .6-3 (2). Due notice of all hearings shall be given to parties in interest
and to the Department of Zoning Administration which Department shall be
permitted to intervene, for and on behalf of the municipality, in all public
hearings;
     .6-3 (3). At any public hearing a representative of the Department of
Zoning Administration and any other interested party may appear in person
or by agent or by attorney, offer evidence and testimony and cross-examine
witnesses;
     .6-3 (4) . All witnesses shall be sworn or shall affirm their testimony in
the manner required in courts of record;
     .6-3 ( 5). All evidence and testimony shall be presented publicly. The
Board may take judicial notice of facts to the same extent and in the same
manner as courts of record and may consider any relevant facts within the
personal knowledge of any member of the Board which are stated into the
record by such member;
     .6-3 (6). For each case or matter heard, the Board shall cause a R(:ord
of its proceedings to be prepared. The record of proceedings shall include all
documents and physical evidence considered in the case together with a trans-
cribed stenographic record of all public proceedings. The transcribed steno-
graphic record shall include, but need not be limited to, the verbatim testimony
offered by all witnesses in the case and all personal knowledge of members of

=== page 12 ===
53   48




          the Board considered by the Board in reaching its decision. The record of
          proceedings shall not include the deliberations or discussions- ofl tJae .Board at
          private or executive sessions but shall show the grounds for each decision and
          the vote of each member upon each question, or, if absent or failing to vote,
          shall indicate such facL The record of proceedings shall be filed immediately
          in the office of the Board and shall be a public record .

          .6-4. POWERS OF THE BOARD.
               Subject to the limitations enumerated herein, the Board shall have and
          exercise the following powers; in the exercise of its powers the Board may
          reverse or affirm, wholly or partly, or may modify the order, requirement,
          decision or determination appealed from and may make such order, require-
          ment, decision or determination as ought to be made and, to that end, shall

=== page 17 ===
  ,6-4 (4), OATHS AND ATTENDANCE OF WITNESSES.
       For the purpose of exercising the powers herein enumerated, the Board
  shall elect a chairman and vice-chairman. The chairman, or, in his absence,
  the vice-chairman, shall administer oaths to or accept affirmations from witnesses
  and may compel the attendance of witnesses. A failure or a refusal to appear
  in response to a subpoena issued by the Board shall constitute a violation of
  this ordinance.
  .6-5.   LIMITATIONS ON POWERS OF BOARD .
  .6-5 ( 1) . CONCURRING VOTE REQUIRED.
       The concurring vote of four members of the Board shall be necessary to
  reverse any order, requirement, decision or determination of any administra-
  tive official or to decide in favor of the applicant on any matter upon which the
  Board is required to pass under this ordinance or to effect any variation in
  this ordinance.
  .6-5 (2). FINDINGS OF FACT.
       Every decision of the Board shall be based upon findings of fact and every
  finding of fact shall be supported in the record of its proceedings. The enumer-
  ated conditions required to exist on any matter upon which the Board is required
  to pass under this ordinance or to effect any variation in this ordinance shall
  be construed as limitations on the power of the Board to act. A mere finding or
  recitation of the enumerated conditions unaccompanied by findings of specific
  facts shall not be deemed findings of fact and shall not be deemed compliance
  with this ordinance.

  .6-5 (3). POWERS STRICTLY CONSTRUED.
       Nothing herein contained shall be construed to empower the Board to
  change the terms of this ordinance, to effect changes in the official map or to
  add to the specific uses permitted in any district. The powers of the Board shall
  be so construed that this ordinance and the official map are strictly enforced.
  .6-6.   APPEALS FROM THE BOARD •
  •6-6 ( 1) • PROCEDURE.
       Any person aggrieved, any taxpayer, the municipality or any officer or
  department of the municipality may have a decision of the Board reviewed in
  the manner provided by rules relating to civil proceedings. No such review
shall be granted unless a petition therefor, duly verified, setting forth that such
decision is illegal, in whole or in part, and specifying the gs«JIJlllUI of the ilkgal;-
ity, is presented to a court of record within thirty days after the filing of the
decision in the office of the Board. The Board shall not be required to return
the original papers acted upon by it, but it shall be sufficient to return certified
or sworn copies thereof or such portions thereof as may be called for. The return
shall concisely set forth such other facts as may be pertinent and material to the
decision appealed from and shall be verified.
,»-6 (2).   EFFECT OF APPEAL.
    The issuance of a writ on a petition hereunder shall not stay proceedings
upon the decision appealed from but the court, on application after notice to
the Board and on due cause shown. may grant a restraining order.

                GROUPS .

.1-1. DESCRIPTION OF DISTRICT.
     This district is composed of certain quiet, low-density residential areas
of the city plus certain open areas where similar residential development appears
likely to occur. The regulations for this district are designed to stabilize and
protect the essential characteristics of the district, to promote and encourage
a suitable environment for family life where children are members of most
families and to prohibit all activities of a commercial nature. To these ends,
development is limited. to a relatively low concentration and permitted uses
are limited basically to single unit dwellings providing homes for the residents




                                                                                              i8

=== page 19 ===
53   54




          plus cer tain additional uses, such as sch ools, parks, churches and certain public
          fa c ilities, which serve the r esiden ts of the district. No home occupations ( includ-
          ing r oom renting) are permitted .
          .1-2.  LIMITATIONS ON EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES.
              Within not more than three years from the date on which the following
          provisions become effective, every use shall be made to comply with the follow-
          ing Limitations:
          .1-2 ( 1). ENCLOSURE OF USES.
               Every use, unless expressly exempted by this ordinance, shall be operated
          in its entirety within a completely enclosed structure; the exemption of a use
          from the requirement of enclosure will be indicated by the phrase "need not
          be enclosed" appearing after any use exempted .
          . 1-2 (2). VOLUME OF SOUND GENERATED.
                Every use, unless expressly exempted by this ordinance, shall be so operated
          that the volume of sound inherently and recurrently generated does not exceed
          fifty-five decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which
          the use is located .
          .1-2 (3). VIBRATION GENERATED.
               Every use shall be so operated that the ground vibration inherently and
          recurrently generated is not perceptible, without instruments, at any point of
          any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located .
          . 1-2 ( 4). EMISSION OF HE AT, GLARE , RADIATION AND FUMES.
                Every use shall be so operated that it does not emit an obn oxious or danger-
          ous degree of heat, glare, radiati on or fumes beyond an y boundary line of the
          Zone Lot on which the use is located .
          .1-2 ( 5). OUTDOOR STORAGE A.N D WASTE DISPOSAL.
               .1-2 ( 5) (a) . No highly flammable or explosive liquids, solids or gases
          shall be stored in bulk above ground. Tanks or drums of fuel directly connect-
          ing with heating devices or appliances located on the same Zone Lot as the tanks
          or drums of fuel are excluded from this provision:
              .1-2 ( 5) (b). All outdoor storage facilities for fuel, raw materials and
          products shall be enclosed by a fence or wall adequate to conceal such facilities
          from adjacent property;
               .1-2 ( 5) ( c). No materials or wastes shall be deposited upon a Zone Lot
          in such form or mann er that they may be transferred off the Zone Lot by natural
          causes or forces ;
               .1-2 ( 5) ( d). All materials or wastes which might cause fumes or dust
          or which constitute a fire hazard or which may be edible by or otherwise be
          attractive to rodents or in ~ects s hall be stored outdoor s o nly in closed containers .
          . 1-3.  PERMITTED USES.
               No land shall he used or occupied and no structure shall be designed,
          erected. altered, used or occupied except for either one only of the following
          Uses by Right and lawful Accessory Uses thereto or for one only of the following
          Uses by Temporary Permit. The number enclosed in parentheses which follows
          any use shall n ot be deemed a limitation of the use but shall be deemed the
          classification of the use for the purposes of off-street parking requirements .
          . 1-3 ( l) . U s Es BY RIGHT.
                The following uses may be operated as Uses by Right:

          . 1-4 (1). ZONE LOT FOR STRUCTURES.
                A separate ground area, herein called the Zone Lot, shall be designated,
          provided and continuously maintained for each structure containing a Use by
          RighL Each Zone Lot shall have at least one front line and shall be occupied
          only by the structure containing a Use by Right and a subordinate structure or
          structures containing only Accessory Uses. The Zone Lot for each structure
          designed or used, either wholly or partly, for residential occupancy shall be not
          less than fifty feet wide at the front setback line for structures, shall contain not
          less than 6,000 square feet and shall provide not less than 3,000 square feet of
          Unobstructed Open Space; provided, however, that the requirement as to mini-
          mum width may be waived on a Zone Lot adjoined on both sides by Zone Lots
          with structures thereon or on a Zone Lot adjoined on one side by a street and
          on the other side by a Zone Lot with a structure or structures thereon. Upon
          application to and approval by the Department of Zoning Administration, the
          boundaries and area of a designated Zone Lot may be amended if full compliance
          with all requirements of this ordinance can be maintained.

          .1-4 (3).   Buu: OF STRUCTURES.

          antennas, chimneys, flues, vents or accessory water tanks) shall project up
          through bulk limits which are defined by planes starting at horizontal lines
          which are co-directional to the front , rear and side lines of the Zone Lot and

          side line and which planes extend up over the Zone Lot at an angle of forty-five
          degrees with respect to the horizontal ( a pitch of one foot additional rise for
          each foot additional setback). (See Graphi c Fig. No. 11 .
          . 1-4 (4). OUTSIDE AREA OF WINDOW EXPOSURE.
                Each legally required window shall have not less than the following amount
          of outside exposure determined in the following manner: From a reference
          point located at the bottom center of the window, extend outward at right angle
          to the window plane, a horizontal sector of 140 degrees, centered on the window,
          with a radius of 10 feet. Within this sector the minimum required outside area
          of exposure for the window shall be any open sector or combination of sectors
          totalling 70 degrees. Applied in the direction of adjoining Zone Lots, the area
          which may be credited as outside area of window exposure. extends to required
          setback lines, regardless of the actual location of structures. ( See Graphic Fig.
          No. 5) .
          . 1-4 ( 5). MINIMUM SIZE OF SINGLE UNIT DWELLINGS.
                Each single unit dw elling shall contain a gross floor area of not less than
          six hundred square feet.

.2-1.  DESCRIPTION OF DISTRICT.
     This district is composed of certain quiet, low-density residential areas of
the city plus certain open areas where similar residential development appears
likely to occur. The regulations for this district are designed to stabilize and
protect the essential characteristics of the district, to promote and encourage
a suitable environment for family life where children are members of most
families and to prohibit all activities of a commercial nature except certain
enumerated Home Occupations (including room renting) controlled by specific
limitations governing the size and extent of such non-residential activities. To
these ends, development is limited to a relatively low concentration and per-
mitted uses are limited basically to single unit dwellings providing homes for
the residents plus certain additional uses, such as schools, parks, churches
and certain public facilities, which serve the residents of the district .
.2-2.  LIMITATIONS ON EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES.
     Within not more than three years from the date on which the following
provisions become effective, every use shall be made to comply with the follow-
ing Limitations :
.2-2 ( 1). ENCLOSURE OF USES.
     Every use, unless expressly exempted by this ordinance, shall be operated
in its entirety within a completely enclosed structure; the exemption of a use

=== page 25 ===
53   60




     from the requirement of enclosure will be indicated by the phr~ "need not
     be enclosed" appearing after any use exempted.                     ·
     .2-2 ( 2 ) .   VOLUME OF SOUND GENERATED.
           Every use, unless expressly exempted by this ordinance, shall be so operated
     that the volume of sound inherently and recurrently generated does not exceed
     fifty-five decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which
     the use is located.
     .2-2 ( 3). VIBRATION GENERATED.
          Every use shall be so operated that the ground vibration inherently and
     recurrently generated is not perceptible, without instruments, at any point of
     any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located.
     .2-2 ( 4). EMISSION OF HEAT, GLARE, RADIATION AND FUMES.
          Every use shall be so operated that it does not emit an obnoxious or
     dangerous degree of heat, glare, radiation or fumes beyond any boundary line
     of the Zone Lot on which the use is located.
     .2-2 ( 5) . OUTDOOR STORAGE AND WASTE DISPOSAL•
          .2-2 ( 5) (a). No highly flammable or explosive liquids, solids or gases
     shall be stored in bulk above ground. Tanks or drums of fuel directly con-
     necting with heating devices or appliances located on the same Zone Lot as
     the tanks or drums of fuel are excluded from this provision;
         .2-2 ( 5) ( b). All outdoor storage facilities for fuel, raw materials and
     products shall be enclosed by a fence or wall adequate to conceal such facilities
     from adjacent property;
         .2-2 ( 5) ( c) . No materials or wastes shall be deposited upon a Zone
     Lot in such form or manner that they may be transferred off the Zone Lot
     by natural causes or forces;
          .2-2 (5) (d). All materials or wastes which might cause fumes or dust
     or which constitute a fire hazard or which may be edible by or otherwise be
     attractive to rodents or insects shall be stored outdoors only in closed containers.
     .2-3.  PERMITTED USES.
          No land shall be used or occupied and no structure shall be designed,
     erected, altered, used or occupied except for either one only of the following
     Uses by Right and lawful Accessory Uses thereto or for one only of the
     following Uses by Temporary Permit. The number enclosed in parentheses
     which follows any use shall not be deemed a limitation of the use but shall
     be deemed the classification of the use for the purposes of off-street parking
     requirements.
     •2-3 (1). USES BY R.lGHT.

                  Lot, all detached- accessory structures and fixtures shall be set in a di.stance i'i
                  not less than five feet and all other structures shall be set in a distance of not
                  less than twenty feet from each rear line of the Zone Lot. If an alley abuts
                  the rear line of the Zone Lot, detached garages and carports opening dir~tly
                  on the alley shall he· set in a distance of not less than five feet from the aller
                  line; detached accessory structures (including garages and carports which do
                  not open directly on the alley) and fixtures for the disposal oi trash and
                  garbage may be located on the alley line and all other structures shall be set
                  in a distance of not less than twenty feet from the center line of the abutting
                  alley; provided, however, that if a Zone Lot has more than one rear line by
                  reason of abutting alleys, the aforesaid setbacks shall be required from only
                  one rear line and the other rear line or lines shall, for the purposes of setbacks,
                  be deemed side lines of the Zone Lot. Whether or not an alley abuts the rear
                  line of the Zone Lot, electric substations, gas regulator stations and utility

pumping stations which do not have a rear entrance may he located on the
rear line of the Zone Lot; if such structures do have a rear entrance they
shall be set in a distance of not less than five feet from the rear line of the
Zone Lot.
     .2-4 ( 2) ( c) . Side Setback. On Zone Lots less than thirty feet in width,
all structures shall be set in a distance of not less than three feet from each
side line of the Zone Lot. On Zone Lots thirty or more feet in width, all
structures shall be set in not less than the following distances from each side
line of the Zone Lot: ( 1) Any structure designed or used, either wholly or
partly, for residential occupancy-five feet; (2) Electric substation, gas regu•
lator station-five feet; (3) Any structure containi.ng any other Use by Right
-ten feet; (4) Any structure containing an Accessory Use-five feet. Any
accessory structure which is located entirely on the rear thirty feet of the Zone
Lot may be located within not less than two feet from each side line of the
Zone Lot .

.2-4 ( 3) .   BULK OF STRUCTURES.
     No part of any structure ( except church spires, church towers, flagpoles,
antennas, chimneys, flues, vents or accessory water tanks) shall project up
through bulk limits which are defined by planes starting at horizontal lines
which are co-directional to the front, rear and side lines of the Zone Lot and
pass through points ten feet above the mid-point of each such front, rear and
side line and which planes extend up over the Zone Lot at an angle of forty.five
degrees with respect to the horizontal ( a pitch of one foot additional rise
for each foot additional setback). (See Graphic Fig. No. 1.)

=== page 31 ===
53   66




          .2-4 (4) . OUTSIDE AREA OF WINDOW EXPOSURE.
                Each legally required window shall have not less than the following
          amount of outside exposure determined in the following m anner· 1f rom a refer-
          ence point located at the bottom center of the window, extend outward at
          right angle to the window plane, a horizontal sector of 140 degrees, centered
          on the window, with a radius of 10 feet. Within this sector the minimum
          required outside area of exposure for the window shall be any open sector or
          combination of sectors totalling 70 degrees. Applied in the direction of ad-
          joining Zone Lots, the area which may be credited as outside area of window
          exposure, extends to required setback lines, regardless of the actual location
          of structures. (See Graphic Fig. No. 5.)
          .2-4 ( 5). MINIMUM SIZE OF SINGLE UNIT DWELLINGS.
               Each single unit dwelling shall contain a gross floor area of not less than
          six hundred square feet .
          .2-5.   PERMITTED SIGNS.
               The provisions of Article 613. Permitted Signs shall be in full force and
          effect in this district.
          .2-6.   OFF-STREET PARKING REQUIREMENTS.
               The provisions of Article 614. Off-Street Parking Requirements shall be
          in full force and effect in this district.
          .2-7.   OFF-STREET LOADING REQUIREMENTS.
               The provisions of Article 615. Off-Street Loading Requirements shall be
          in full force and effect in this district.
          .2-8. SPECIAL ZONE LOT PLAN FOR PLANNED BUILDING GROUPS.
              The provisions of Article 616. Special Zone Lot Plan for Planned Building
          Groups shall be of no force and effect in this district.

        .3-U.          SPECIAL ZONE LOT PLAN FOR PLANNED BUILDING
                       GROUPS .

.3-1.  DESCRIPTION OF DISTRICT.
      This district is composed of certain medium-density residential areas of
the city representing a compatible co-mingling of single unit and multiple unit
dwellings, plus certain open areas where similar residential development appears
likely to occur. The regulations for this district are designed to stabilize and
protect the essential characteristics of the district, to promote and encourage
a suitable environment for family life where children are members of.. many
families and to prohibit all activities of a commercial nature except certain
enumerated Home Occupations (including room renting ) controlled by specific
limitations governing the size and extent of such non-residential activities. To
these ends, dev elopment is limited to a medium concentration and permitted
uses are typically single and two-unit dwellings, courts and garden apartments




                                                                                    32

=== page 33 ===
53   68




          providing homes for the residents plus certain additional uses, such as schools,
          parks, churches and certain public facilities, which serve the residents of the
          district. However, large apartments, with corresponding proportions of open
          space. also may be developed under prescribed standards of density and open
          space .
          . 3-2. LIMITATIONS OF EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES.
               Within not more than three years from the date on which the following
          provisions become effective, every use shall be made to comply with the follow-
          ing Limitations:
          .3-2 (1). ENCLOSURE OF ll~r,~.
               EYery use, unlc,s expressly exempted by this ordinance. shall be operated
          in its entirety within a completely enclosed structure: the exemption of a use
          from the requirement of enclosure will be indicated by the phrase "need not
          be enclosed" appearing after any use exempted .
          .3-2 (2). VOLUME OF ~OlJND GENERATED.
                Every use, unless expressly exempted by this ordinance. shall be so operated
          that the volume of sound inherently and recurrently generated does not exceed
          fifty-five decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which
          the use is located .
          .3-2 I ;3). VIBRATION GENERATED.
               Every use shall be so operated that the ground vibration inherently and
          recurrently generated is not perceptible. without instruments, at any point
          of any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located .
          .3-2 ( 4). EMISSION oF HEAT, GLARE, RADIATION AND Ful\rn,.
               Every use shall be so operated that it does not emit an obnoxious or
          dangerous degree of heat, glare, radiation or fumes beyond any boundary
          line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located .
          .3-2 ( 5).   OUTDOOR STORAGE AND WASTE DISPOSAL •

               .3-2 ( 5) (a). No highly flammable or explosive liquids, solids or gases
          shall be stored in bulk above ground. Tanks or drums of fuel directly connecting
          with heating devices or appliances located on the same Zone Lot as the tanks
          or drums of fuel are excluded from this provision:
              .3-2 ( 5) I b). All outdoor storage facilities for fuel. raw materials and
          products shall be enclosed by a fence or wall adequate to conceal such facilities
          from adj a cent property;
               .3-2 ( 5) ( c). No materials or wastes shall be deposited upon a Zone Lot
          in such form or manner that they may be transferred off the Zone Lot by
          natural causes or forces:
               .3-2 ( 5) ( d). All materials or wastes which might cause fumes or dust
          or which constitute a fire hazard or which may be edible by or otherwise be
          attractive to rodents or insects shall be stored outdoors only in closed containers .
          .3-3.  PERMITTED USES.
               No land shall be used or occupied and no structure shall be designed.
          erected. altered, used or occupied except for either one or several of the follow-
          ing Uses by Right and lawful Accessory Uses thereto or for one or several of
          the following Uses by Temporary Permit. The number enclosed in parentheses
          which follows any use shall not be deemed a limitation of the use but shall

          .3-3 (2). USES BY TEMPORARY PERMIT.
                Upon application to and issuance by the Department of Zoning Adminis•
          tration of a permit therefor, the following uses may he operated as Uses by
          Temporary Permit:
               .3-3 (2) (a). Bazaar and/or carnival; provided, however, that each
          permit shall he valid for a period of not more than three days and shall not       _)
          he renewed for more than three successive periods; and provided, further, that
          a period of at least ninety days shall intervene between the termination of one
          permit and the issuance of another permit for the same location; need not he
          enclosed;
               .3-3 (2) (h). Newspaper distribution station: a newspaper distribution
          station operated in accordance with all of the following standards: Shall he
          maintained in a sightly condition with all papers, waste wrappers and news-
          papers stored, handled and distributed within a completely enclosed structure
          until removed from the premises; shall provide adequate on the premises storage
          for bicycles and shall prohibit unnecessary loitering on or about the premises;
          provided, however, that each permit shall he valid for a period of not more
          than one calendar year hut may he renewed; and provided, further, that a
          failure to comply with any of the standards herein set forth shall he cause for
          revocation of any permit;
               .3-3 (2) (c). Non-commercial concrete hatching plant, both incidental
          and necessary to construction in the district ; provided, however, that each
          permit shall he valid for a period of not more than six calendar months and
          shall not he renewed for more than six successive periods at the same location;
                .3-3 (2) (d). Parking lot designated for a special event; provided, how-
          ever, that each permit shall he valid only for the duration of the designated
          special event; and provided, further, that if the designated special event is a
          seasonal activity, the permit may he valid for the entire season hut shall he
          restricted in use to designated dates and times during which the event is
          occurring; need not he enclosed;
               .3-3 (2) (e). Temporary building or yard for construction materials
          and/or equipment, both incidental and necessary to construction in the district;
          provided, however, that each permit shall he valid for a period of not more
          than six calendar months and shall not he renewed for more than four successive
          periods at the same location; need not he enclosed;
               .3-3 (2) (f). Temporary office, both incidental and necessary for the
          sale or rental of real property in the district; provided, however, that each
          permit shall he valid for a period of not more than six calendar months and
          shall not he renewed for more than four successive periods at the same location.

      may project five feet into front setback space, ten feet into rear setback space

      •3-4 ( 3). BULK OF STRUCTURES.
            No part of any structure (except church spires, church towers, flagpoles,
      antennas, chimneys, flues, vents or accessory water tanks) shall project up
      through bulk limits which are defined by planes starting at horizontal lines
      which are co-directional to the front, rear and side lines of the Zone Lot
      and pass through points ten feet above the midpoint of each such front, rear
      and side line and which planes extend up over the Zone Lot at an angle of
      forty-five degrees with respect to the horizontal (a pitch of one foot additional
      rise for each foot additional setback). (See Graphic Fig. No. 1.)
      .3-4 ( 4). OUTSIDE AREA OF WINDOW ExPosURE.
           Each legally required window shall have not less than the following amount
      of outside exposure determined in the following manner: From a reference
      point located at the bottom center of the window, extend outward at right
      angle to the window plane, a horizontal sector of 140 degrees, centered on
      the window, with a radius of 10 feet. Within this sector the minimum required
      outside area of exposure for the window shall he any open sector or combina-
      tion of sectors totalling 70 degrees. Applied in the direction of adjoining Zone
      Lots, the area which may be credited as outside area of window exposure,
      extends to required setback lines, regardless of the actual location of structures.
       (See Graphic Fig. No. 5.)
      .3-4 (5).   MINIMUM SIZE OF SINGLE UNIT DWELLINGS AND OTHER DWELUNG
                  UNITS.
          Each single unit dwelling and each of the following dwelling units shall
      contain a gross floor area of not less than six hundred square feet: (1) Any
      dwelling unit having an individual outside entrance at ground level; (2)
      Any dwelling unit the floor of which dwelling unit is at ground level or is
      below ground level or is less than ten feet above ground level.
      . 3-5. PERMITTED SIGNS.
          The provisions of Article 613. Permitted Signs shall be in full force
      and effect in this district.
      •3-6.  OFF-STREET PARKING REQUIREMENTS.
           The provisions of Article 614. Off-Street Parking Requirements shall be
      in full force and effect in this districL
      .3-7.   OFF-STREET LOADING REQUIREMENTS.
           The provisions of Article 615. Off-Street Loading Requirements shall be
      in full force and effect in this district.
      •3-8.SPECIAL ZONE LOT PLAN FOR PLANNED BUILDING GROUPS.
          The provisions of Article 616. Special Zone Lot Plan for Planned Building
      Groups shall he in full force and effect in this district.

•4-1.      DESCRIPTION OF DISTRICT.
     This district is composed of certain high-density residential areas of the
city plus certain open areas where similar development appears likely to occur.
The regulations for this district are designed to stabilize and protect the essen-
tial characteristics of the district, to promote and encourage, insofar as com-
patible with the intensity of land use, a suitable environment for family life
composed predominantly of an adult population and to prohibit all activities
of a commercial nature except certain enumerated Home Occupations (including
room renting) controlled by specific limitations governing the size and extent
of such non-residential activities. To these ends, this district is protected

=== page 41 ===
53   76




          against encroachment of general commercial or industrial uses while the regu-
          lations permit high-density development consistent with the high concentration
          of persons and land valuation. All residential types of structures, including
          institutions such as hospitals and ~ollegetf, an pfmll.iild:I but the predominant
          structural types will he apartments and multiple unit dwellings plus certain
          additional uses such as schools, parks, churches and certain public facilities
          which serve the residents of the district.
          •4-2. LIMITATIONS ON EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES.
               Within not more than three years from the date on which the following
          provisions become effective, every use shall be made to comply with the follow-
          ing Limitations:
          .4-2 (1). °ENCLOSURE OF USES.
               Every use, unless expressly exempted by this ordinance, shall be operated
          in its entirety within a completely enclosed structure; the exemption of a use
          from the requirement of enclosure will he indicated by the phrase "need not
          be enclosed" appearing after any use exempted.
          .4-2 (2). VOLUME OF SOUND GENERATED.
               Every use, unless expressly exempted by this ordinance, shall be so operated
          that the volume of sound inherently and recurrently generated does not exceed
          sixty decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the
          use is located•
          .4-2 (3). VIBRATION GENERATED.
               Every use shall be so operated that the ground vibration inherently and
          recurrently generated is not perceptible, without instruments, at any point of
          any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located.
          .4-2 (4). EMISSION OF HEAT, GLARE, RADIATION AND FUMES.
               Every _uSe shall he so operated that it does not emit an obnoxious or
          dangerous degree of heat, glare, radiation or fumes beyond any boundary line
          of the Zone Lot on which the use is located.
          .4-2 ( 5).   OUTDOOR STORAGE AND WASTE DISPOSAL.
               .4-2 (5) (a). No highly flammable or explosive liquids, solids or gases
          shall he stored in bulk above ground. Tanks or drums of fuel directly con-
          necting with heating devices or appliances located on the same Zone Lot as the
          tanks or drums of fuel are excluded from this provision;
              .4-2 ( 5) (b). All outdoor storage facilities for fuel, raw materials and
          products shall he enclosed by a fence or wall adequate to conceal such facilities
          from adjacent property;
               .4-2 (5) (c). No materials or wastes shall be deposited upon a Zone
          Lot i_n such form or manner that they may be transferred off the Zone Lot by
          natural causes or forces;
               .4-2 ( 5) ( d) . All materials or wastes which might cause fumes or dust
          or which constitute a fire hazard or which may be edible by or otherwise he
          attractive to rodents· or insects shall he stored outdoors only in closed containers.
          •4-3: PERMITTED USES.
               No land shall be used or occupied and no structure shall be designed,
          erected, altered, used or occupied except for either one or several of the follow-

      •4-4 (2) b). Reor Setback. If no alley abuts the rear line of the Zone
Lot, all detached accessory structures and fixtures shall be set in a distance of
not less than five feet and all other structures shall be set in a distance of not
lea than twenty feet from each rear line of the Zone LoL If an alley abuts the
rear Hne of the Zone Lot, detached garages and carports opening directly on
the alley shall be set in a distance of not less than five feet from the a.Hey
line ; detached accessory structures (including garages and carports which do
not open directly on the alley) and fixtures for the disposal of trash and
garbage may be located on the alley line and all other structures shall be
set in a distance of not less than twenty feet from the center line of the abutting
alley; provided, however, that if a Zone Lot has more than one rear line by
reason of abutting alleys, the aforesaid setbacks shall be required from only
one rear line and the other rear line or lines shall, for the purposes of setbacks,
be deemed side lines of the Zone Lot. Whether or not an alley abuts the rear
line of the Zone Lot, electric substations, gas regulator stations and utility
pumping stations which do not have a rear entrance may be located on the
rear line of the Zone Lot; if such structures do have a rear entrance they
shall be set in a distance of not less than five feet from the rear line of the
Zone Lot.

.4-4 (4). M.ulll(UM Gaoss FLOOR AREA IN STRUCTURES.
     The sum total of the gross Boor area of all s~ctures on a Zon.e Lot,
excluding garage space, shall not be greater than three times the area of the
Zone Lot on which the structures are located.
.4-4 ( 5). 01.rrsIDE AREA OF "WINDOW EXPOSURE.
     Each legally required window shall have not less than the followin~ amount
of outside exposure determined in the following manner: From a reference
point located at the bottom center of the window, extend outward at right
angl9, to the Qdow plane, a horizontal sector of 140 degrees, centered on
the window, .       a radius of fifteen feet. Within this sector the minimum
required outside area of exposure for the window shall be any open sector or
combination of sectors totalling 70 degrees. Applied in the direction of
adjoining Zone Lots, the area which may be credit~ as outside area of window
exposure, extends to required setback lines, regardless of the actual location
of structures. (See Graphic Fig. No. 5.)
.4-4 (6).  MINIMUM SrzE OF SINGLE UNIT DwELUNGS AND OTHER DwELUNG
           UNITS.                            .

            .5-1. DESCRIPTION OF DISTRICT.
                 This district is composed of certain of the very high or highest-density
            residential areas of the city, ordinarily located between residential and com-
            mercial areas, plus certain open areas where similar development appears likely
            to occur. The regulations for this district are designed to stabilize and protect
            the essential characteristics of the district, to promote and encourage, insofar
            as compatible with the high intensity of land use, a suitable environment for
            family life ~osed almost entirely of an adult population, children are rarely
            present, ~ to permit certain commercial uses of a character unlikely to
            to devel general concentration of traffic, crowds of customers and general
            outdoor a ertising. To these ends, retail activity is sharply limited and this
            district is protected against encroachment of general commercial or industrial
            uses while the regulations permit high-density development consistent with
            the high concentration of persons and land valuation. All residential types of
            structures, for both permanent and transient occupancy and including institu-
            tions, are permitted plus structures for commercial uses conforming to the
            pattern of the districL
            .5-2.  LIMITATIONS ON EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES.
                Within not more than three years from the date on which the following
            provisions become effective, every use shall be made to comply with the follow-
            ing Limitations:




                                                                                                49

=== page 50 ===
                                                                                     53   85




.5-2 (1).     MCLOSURE OF USES.
     Every use, unless expressly exempted by this ordinance, shall be operated
in its entirety within a completely enclosed structure ; the e~tion of .a use
from the requirement of enclosure will be indicated by the phrase "need not be
enclosed" appearing after any use exempted.
•5-2 (2). VOLUME OF SOUND GENERATED.
     Every use, unless expressly exempted by this ordinance, shall be so
operated that the volume of sound inherently and recurrently generated does
not exceed sixty decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone Lot
on which the use is located.
•5-2 (3). VIBRATION GENERATED.
     Every use shall he so operated that the ground vibration inherently and
recurrently generated is not perceptible, without instruments, at any point of
any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located .
•5-2 (4). EMISSION OF HEAT, GLARE, RADIATION AND FUMES.
     Every use shall be so operated that it does not emit an obnoxious or
dangerous degree of heat, glare, radiation or fumes beyond any boundary
line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located.
.5-2 ( 5) . OUTDOOR STORAGE AND WASTE DISPOSAL•
     .5-2 (5) (a). No highly flammable or explosive liquids, solids or gases
shall be stored in bulk above ground. Tanks or drums of fuel directly con-
necting with heating devices or appliances located on the same Zone Lot as
the tanks or drums of fuel are excluded from this provision;
    .5-2 ( 5) (b). All outdoor storage facilities for fuel, raw materials and
products shall be enclosed by a fence or wall adequate to conceal such facilities
from adjacent property;
     .5-2 ( 5) ( c) . No materials or wastes shall be deposited upon a Zone Lot
in such form or manner that they may be transferred off the Zone Lot by
natural causes or forces;
     .5-2 (5) (d). All materials or wastes which might cause fumes or dust
or which constitute a fire hazard or which may be edible Jw or otherwise he
attractive to rodents or insects shall be stored outdoors only in closed con-
tainers.
.5-3.    PERMITTED USES.
     No land shall be used or occupied and no structure shall be designed,
erected, altered, used or occupied except for either one or several of the follow-
ing Uses by Right and lawful Accessory Uses thereto or for one or several
of the following Uses by Temporary Permit. The number enclosed in paren-
theses which follows any use shall not be deemed a limitation of the use hut
shall he deemed the classification of the use for the purposes of off-street
parking requirements.
.5-3 (1). USES BY RIGHT.
     The following uses may he operated as Uses by Right:

                    .5-4 (2) (h). Rear Setback. If no alley abuts the rear liae. of ~ Zone
              l,ot all detached accessory structures and fixtures shall he set in a distaDce of
              not'less than five feet and all other structures shall be ¥tin a d ~ of pot
              less than twenty feet from each rear line of the Zone Lot. If an .. ~ley ah~

              line; detached accessory structures ( including garages and carports wnich
              do not open directly on the alley) and fixtures for the disposal of trash and

              pumping stations which do not have a rear entrance may be located on the
              rear line of the Zone Lot; if such structures do have a rear entrance they shall
              he set in a distance of not less than five feet from the rear line 0£ the Zone Lot.

                    . 5-4 (2) (c). Side Setback. On Zone Lots less than thirty feet in width,
              all structures shall be set in a distance of not less than three feet from each side •
              line of the Zone Lot. On Zone Lots thirty or more feet in width, electric sub.
              stations and gas regulator stations shall he set in a distance of not less than five
              feet and all other structures shall he set in a distance of not less than seven feet .
              and six inches from each side line of the Zone Lot. Any accessory structure
              which is located entirely on the rear thirty feet of the Zone Lot may he located •
              within not less than two feet from each side line of the Zone Lot.

    •6-1.  DESCRIPTION OF DISTRICT.
         This district is composed of certain land and structures used primarily to
    provide office space for services related to dental and medical care and for office
    type services either for the residents of nearby residential areas or characterized
    by a low volume of direct daily customer contact. The volume of vehicular traffic
    is usually low. Usually this district is locatM away from main arterial high-
    ways, characteristically ·is small in sfze and fs situated near major hospitals, be-
    tween large business areas and residential areas or entirely sw;rounded by resi-
    dential districts. The regulations are designed · to permit development of the
    enumerated functions, limited by standards designed to protect the aliutting or
    surrounding residential districts. To these ends, the regulations establish stand-
    ards comparable to the standards for low-density residential districts, resulting in
    similar building bullc and retaining the low concentration of pedestrian and vehi-
    cular traffic.

              .6-2 Lll\tilTATIONS ON EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES.
                . . Within not more. than three years from the date on which the following pro-
              visions become effective, every use shall he made l!O oomp[y with the following
              Limitations:
              .6-2 (1). ENCLOSURE OF UsES.                                                           _J
                   Every use, unless expressly exempted .by this ordinance, shall he operated in
              its entirely within a completely enclosed structure; the exemption of a use from       -.
              the requirement of enclosure will he indicated by the phrase "need not he en-
              closed" appearing after any use exempted.              •
              .6-2 (2). VoLUME OF SouND GENERATED.
                    Every use, unless expressly exempted by this ordinance, shall he so operated
              that the volume of sound inherently and recurrently generated does not exceed
               sixty decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the use
              is located.
              .6-2 (3). VIBRATION GENERATED.
                   Every use shall he so operated that the ground vibration inherently and re-
              currently generated is not perceptible, without instruments, at any point of any
              boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located.
              .6-2 (4). EMISSION OF HEAT, GLARE, RADIATION AND FUMES.
                   Every use shall be so operated that it does not emit an obnoxious or danger-
              ous degree of heat, glare, radiation or fumes beyond any boundary line of the
              Zone Lot on which the use is located .
              .6-2 ( 5) . OUTDOOR STORAGE AND WASTE DISPOSAL.
                   .6-2 ( 5) (a). No highly flammable or explosive liquids, solids or gases
              shall he stored in bulk above ground. Tanks or drums of fuel directly connect-
              ing with heating devices or appliances located on the same Zone Lot as the tanks
              or drums of fuel are excluded from this provision;
                  .6-2 ( 5) (b). All outdoor storage facilities for fuel, raw materials and
              products shall be enclosed by a fence or wall adequate to conceal such facilities
              from adjacent property;
                   .6-2 (5) (c). No materials or wastes shall he deposited upon a Zone Lot
              in such form or manner that they may be transferred off the Zone Lot by natural
              causes or forces;
                    .6-2 (5) (d). All materials or wastes which might cause fumes or dust
              or which constitute a fire hazard or which may he edible by or otherw ise be at-
              tractive to rodents or insects shall he stored outdoors onlpiu. closed containers.
              .6-3. PERMITIED USES.
                    No land shall be used or occupied and no structure shall be designed,
              erected, altered, used or occupied except for either one or several of.the following
              Uses by Right and lawful Accessory Uses thereto or for one or several of the
              following Uses by Temporary Permit. The number enclosed in parentheses which
              follows any use shall not be deemed a limitation of the use but shall be deemed
              the classification of the use for the purposes of off-street palking requirements.
              .6-3 (1). USES BY RIGHT.
                  · The following uses may be operated as Uses by Right:

     sociated with the operation of the Use by Right;
          .6-3 ( 3) ( b) . Is operated and maintained under the same ownership and
     on the same Zone Lot as the Use by Right;
          .6-3 ( 3) ( c) • Does not include structures or structural features incon-
     sistent with the Use bv Right;
         .6-3 ( 3) ( d) • Does not include residential occupancy except by employees
     employed on the premises and the immediate families of such ~ployees;
          .6-3 ( 3) (e) . Does not utilize a gross floor area in excess of ten per cent

          .6-3 (3) (f). That the gross floor area utilized by all Accessory Uses (ex-

    .6-4. PERMITTED STRUCTURES
    .6-4 (1). ZoNE LOT FOR STRUCTURES.
         A separate ground area, herein called the Zone Lot, shall he designated, pro-
    vided and continuously maintained for each structure containing a Use hy Right.
    Each Zone Lot shall have at least one front line and shall be occupied only by the
    structure containing a Use by Right and one subordinate structure containing
    only Accessory Uses. Upon application to and approval by the Department of
    Zoning Administration, the boundaries and area of a designated Zone Lot may
    be amended if full compliance with all requirements of this ordinance can be
    maintained.
    •6-4 (2). LOCATION OF STRUCTURES.
         All structures shall be set in a distance of not less than ten feet from each
    front and rear line of the Zone Lot; provided, however, that electric substations,
    gas regulator stations, utility pumping stations, detached structures and fixtures
    for the disposal of trash or garbage may be located within five feet of the rear
    line of the Zone Lot; and provided, further, that if a Zone Lot has more than
    one rear line hy reason of abutting alleys, the aforesaid setbacks shall be re-
    quired from only one rear line. The space resulting from the foregoing setbacks
    shall be open and unobstructed except for use as open off-street parking space;
    provided, however:
          .6-4 (2) (a). Belt courses, sills and lintels may pioject eighteen inches
     into front and rear setback spaces;
         .6-4 ( 2) ( b) . Cornices, eaves and gl#en may project three feet into
    front setback. space and five feet into rear setback space;
         .6-4 (2) (c). Outside stairways may project five feet into front setback
    space and ten feet into rear setback space;
         .6-4 (2) ( d).   Unwalled porches, terraces and balconies may extend five
    feet into front and rear setback spaces;

          .6-4 ( 5) . OUTSIDE AREA OF WINDOW EXPOSURE.
               Each legally required window shall have not less than the following amount

          located at the bottom center of the window, extend outward at right angle to the
          window plane, a horizontal sector of 140 degrees, centered on the window, with a
          radius of ten feet. Within this sector the minimum required outside area of ex-
          posure for the window shall be any open sector or combination of sectors totalling
          70 degrees. Applied in the direction of adjoining Zone Lots, the area which may
          be credited as outside area of window exposure, extends to required setback lines,
          regardless of the actual location of structures. (See Graphic Fig. No. 5.)

                 TION.
     .7-12 •     B-2 DISTRICTS PREVIOUSLY ESTABLISHED .
 .7-1. DESCRIPTION OF DISTRICT.
       This district is composed of certain land and structures used primarily to pro-
 vide the retailing of commodities classed by merchants as "convenience goods,"
  such as groceries and drugs, and the furnishing of certain personal services,
  such as the cutting of hair and repairing of shoes, satisfying the daily and weekly
 household or personal needs of the residents of abutting residential neighbor-
 hoods. Purchasing in the district is primarily by women, frequently accompanied
 by their children, and the volume of pedestrian traffic in proportion to auto-
 mobile traffic entering the district is much higher than in other retail Business
 districts. This district is located either on or away from main arterial highways,
 characteristically is small, almost always is entirely surrounded by residential
 districts and is located at a convenient walking distance from the edges of the
 surrounding residential districts it is designed to serve. The regulations are de-
 signed to permit development of the enumerated functions, limited by standards
 designed to protect the abutting or surrounding residential districts. To these
 ends, the regulations establish standards comparable to the standards for low-
 density residential districts, resulting in similar building bulk and retaining the
 relatively low concentration of vehicular traffic as compared to other retail busi-
 ness districts. Of great importance is the necessity that the areas selected for this
 district actually be developed as permitted. Otherwise, the surrounding residential
 area will lack the retail services needed for stable neighborhoods. To insure the
 provision of essential retail services to a neighborhood, the privilege of providing
 these services is matched by the responsibility and duty to so provide. Conse-
 quently, the right to develop this district may be lost if actual development does
 not occur.
 .7-2. LIMITATION~ ON EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES.
      Within not more than three years from the date on which the following pro-
 visions become effective, every use shall be made to comply with the following
 Limitations:
 .7-2 (1).     ENCLOSURE OF USES.
      Every use, unless expressly exempted by this ordinance, shall be operated in
 its entirety within a complietely enclosed structure; the ,exemption of a use from
 the requirement of enclosure will be indicated by the phrase "need not be en-
 closed" appearing after any use exempted.
 .7-2 (2).     VOLUME OF SOUND GENERATED.
       Every use, unless expressly exempted by this ordinance, shall be so operated
 that the volume of sound inherently and .recurrently generated does not exceed
 sixty decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the use
 is located.

    .'l-~ ~3). YIBJL\'IION Gf;m:a~~
-        Every use shall be so operated that the ground vibration inhe~ and ti,;
    currently generated is not perceptible, without instruments, at &Jl1 point of any
    boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located.

    . 7-2 (4). EMISSION OF HEAT, GLARE, RADIATION AND FUMES.
          Every use shall be so operated that it does not emit an obnoxious or danger-
    ous degree of heat, glare, radiation or fumes beyond any boundary line of the
    Zone Lot on which the use is located.

    .7-2 (5). OUTDOOR STORAGE AND WASTE DISPOSAL•
         .7-2 (5) (a). No highly flammable or explosive liquids, solids or gases
    shall be stored in balk above ground. Tanks or drums of fuel directly connecting
    with heating devices or appliances located on the same Zone Lot as the tanks or
    drums of fuel are excluded from this provision;

        .7-2 (5) (b). All outdoor storage facilities for fuel, raw materials and
    products shall be enclosed by a fence or wall adequate to conceal such facilities
    from adjacent property;

         .7-2 (5) (c). No materials or wastes shall be deposited upon a Zone Lot
    in such form or manner that they may be transferred off the Zone Lot by natural
    causes or forces;                 ·

         .7-2 (5) (d). All materials or wastes which might cause fumes or dust or
    which constitute a fire hazard or which may be edible by or .otherwise be at•
    tractive to rodents or insects shall be stored outdoors only in closed containers.

    . 7-3. PERMITTED USES.
         No land shall be used or occupied and no structure shall be designed,
    erected, altered, used or occupied except for either one or several of the follow-
    ing Uses by Right and lawful Accessory Uses thereto or for one or several of
    the following Uses by Temporary Permit. The number enclosed in parentheses
    which follows any use shall not be deemed a limitation of the use but shall be
    deemed the classification of the use for the purposes of off-street parking re-
    quirements.
