  . 7-3 (3). ACCESSORY USES.
        Incidental only to a Use by Right, any use which complies with all of the fol-
  lowing conditions may be operated, either in a detached structure or in the
  structure containing the Use by Right, as an Accessory Use:
       . 7-3 ( 3) (a) . Is clearly incidental and customary to and commonly
  associated with the operation of the Use by Right;
       .7-3 (3) (b). Is operated and maintained under the same ownership and
  on the same Zone Lot as the Use by Right;
        .7-3 (3) (c). Does not include structures or structural features in-
  consistent with the Use by Right;
        .7-3 (3) (d). Does not include residential occupancy except by employees
  employed on the premises and the immediate families of such employees;
        . 7-3 ( 3) ( e). Does not utilize a gross floor area in excess of ten per cent
  of the gross floor area utilized by the Use by Right; provided, however,
        .7-3 (3) (f). That the gross floor area utilized by all Accessory Uses
  (except garages, loading docks and company dining rooms) to all Uses by Right
  in the same structure shall not be in excess of ten per cent of the gross floor area
  utilized by all of the Uses by Right.
  .7-4.     PERMITIED STRUCTURES .
  . 7-4 (1). ZONE LOT FOR STRUCTURES.
        A separate ground area, herein called the Zone Lot, shall be designated, pro-
  vided and continuously maintained for each structure containing a Use by
  Right. Each Zone Lot shall have at least one front line and shall be occupied only
  by the structure containing a Use by Right and one subordinate structure
  containing only Accessory Uses. Upon application to and apprqv,al by the De-
  partment of Zoning Administration, the boundaries and area of a designated
  Zone Lot may he amended if full compliance with all requirements of this
  ordinance can be maintained.

           .7-4 (3). BULK OF STRUC~
                 No part of any structure (ex<cept church spires, church towers, ~ s ,
           antennas, chimneys, flues, vents or accessory water tanks) shall project up
            through hulk limits which are defined by planes starting at horizontal lines which
            are co-directional to the front and rear lines of the Zone Lot and pass through
           points ten feet above the midpoint of each such front and rear line and which
           planes extend up over the Zone Lot at an angle of forty-five degrees with respect
            to the horizontal ( a pitch of one foot additional rise for each foot additional
           setback); provided, however, that the highest part of any lawful sign and any
            flood light on a pole may he at least twenty-five feet above the ground level at
           such sign or pole. (See Graphic Fig. No. 2)

           .7-4 (4). MAXIMUM GROSS FLOOR AREA IN STRUCTURES.
                The sum total of the gross floor area of all structures on a Zone Lot, exclud-
           ing the gross floor area of off-street parking garage space, shall not exceed the
           area of the Zone Lot on which the structures are located .
           . 7-4 (5). OUTSIDE AREA OF WINDOW EXPOSURE.
                 Each legally required window shall have not less than the following amount
           of outside exposure determined in the following manner: From a reference
           point located at the bottom center of the window, extend outward at right angle
           to the window plane, a horizontal sector of 140 degrees, centered on the window,
           with a radius of 10 feet. Within this sector the minimum required outside.area
           of exposure for the window shall be any open sector or combination of sectors
           tobilling 70 de~. Applied in the direction of adjoining Zone Lots, the area
           which may be credited as outside area of window exposure, extends to re-
            quired setback lines, regardless o~ the actual location of structures. ( See Graphic
           Fig. No. 5)

.7-9.  EFFECT OF DISTRICT DESIGNATION.
     The designation of an area as a B-2 District shall cause the conditions and·  re-
quirements set forth in Section .7-10. hereof to come into full force and effect.
Such designation shall not create any right to erect or alter any structures or
to apply for a permit therefor, nor shall such right exist until the filing, approval,
registration and recording of a B-2 District Development Plan as hereinafter
set forth. Whenever a failure to comply with the provisions of Section .7-10.
occurs, no permits shall be issued for the erection or alteration of any structures
in the district and all issued permits therefor shall he terminated and cancelled
until the area included within the district has been examined by Council and
zoned or rezoned for its most appropriate use. Any applicant for the designa-
tion of an area as a B-2 District and any person, firm or corpo'ration who
applies for a permit to alter or erect a structure in the district, whether under
disability or otherwise and including the successors and assigns thereof, shall
he deemed conclusively to have assented to all of these conditions.

           8-1.      DESCRIPTION OF DISTRICT.
                   This district is composed of certain land and structures used lrimarily to
           ~~i~~ :::h r::a;~i:c~r~;s ~~!r~:~!~~d~l= g~~J£&~U:O~~~
           ances and home ~ 1'illlaion gooft!," such.,.,, ..lotl.Lc              ~L•--~- --
           mobiles, and the furnishing of certain personal services, such as cutting of hair
           and repairing of shoes, and of other major personal services, such as banks
           and theaters, satisfying all household and personal needs of the residents of
           abutting large residential communitie". Purchasing in the district is primarily
           by adults of both sexes, sometimes accompanied by their children, and the
           volume of pedestrian traffic in proportion to automobile traffic entering the
           district is relatively low. This district is usually located on a major arterial
           highway at or near the intersection with one or more other major arterial high-
           ways so that it is accessible from all directions, characteristically is large, almost
           always is entirely surrounded by Residential or B-1 Districts and is located at a
           convenient driving distance from the edges of the surrounding residential
           districts it is designed to serve. The regulations are designed to permit develop-
           ment of the enumerated functions, limited by standards designed to protect
           the abutting or surrounding residential districts. To these ends, the regulations
           establish standards comparable to the standards for low density residential
           districts, resulting in similar building bulk and retaining as low a concentration
           of vehicular traffic as is compatible with the functioning of the district. Of great
           importance is the necessity that the areas selected for this district actually be
           developed as permitted. Otherwise, the surrounding residential areas will lack
           the major retail services needed for stable communities. To insure the provision
           of essential major retail services to a community, the privilege of providing
           these services is matched by the responsibility and duty to so provide. Conse-
           quently, the right to develop this district may he lost if actual development
           does not occur.
           •8-2.  LIMITATIONS ON EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES.
                Within not more than three years from the date on which the following pro-
           visions become effective, every use shall be made to comply with the following
           Limitations:
           .~2 (1) . . ENCLOSURE OF USES.
                 Every use, unless expressly exempted by this ordinance, shall be operated
           in its entirety within a completely enclosed structure; the exemption of a use
           from the requirement of enclosure will he indicated by the phrase "need not
           be enclosed" appearing after any use exempted.
           .8-2 (2). VOLUME OF SOUND GENERATED.
                Every use, unless expressly exempted by this ordinance, shall be so operated
           that the volume of !!ound inherently and recurrently generated does not ex-
           ceed seventy decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone Lot on
           which the use is !ocated.
           .8-2 (3). VIBRATION GENERATED.
                Every use shall he so operated that the ground vibration inherently and
           recurrently generated is not perceptible, without instruments, at any point of any
           boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the use is Jocated.
           .8-2 (4). EMISSION OF HEAT, GLARE, RADIATION AND FUMES.
                Every use shall he so operated that it does not emit an obnoxious or dan-
           gerous degree of heat, glare, radiation and fumes beyond any boundary line
           of the Zone Lot on which the use is located.

         Right. Each Zone Lot shall have at lehst one front Hne and shall be occupied

         containing only Actessory Uses. Upon application to and approval by the
         Department of Zoning Administration, the boundaries and area of designated
         Zone Lot may he amende~ if full compliance with all requirements of this
         ordinance can be maintained .
         .8-4 (2). LOCATION OF STRUCTURES.
              All structures shall be set in a distance of not less than five feet from each
         front and rear line of the Zone Lot and a distance of not less than five feet
         from each side line of the Zone Lor which ab'ats a street or alley right-of-way.
         The space resulting-from the foregoing setbacks shall be open and unobstructed
         except for use as open off-street parking space; provided; however,

  .8-4 (4). MAXIMUM GROSS FLOOR AREA IN STRUCTURES.
       The sum total of the gross floor area of all structures on a Zone Lot,
  excluding the gross floor area of off-street parking garage space, shall not
  exceed the area of the Zone Lot on which the structures are located.

  .8-4 (5). OUTSIDE AREA OF WINDOW EXPOSURE.
       Each legally required window shall have not less than the following amount
  of outside exposure determined in the following manner: From a reference
  point located at the bottom center of the window, extend outward at right
  angle to the window plane, a horizontal sector of 140 degrees, centered on
  the window, with a radius of 10 feet. Within this sector the minimum required
  outside area of exposure for the window shall be any open sector or combination
  of sectors totalling 70 aegrees, Applied in the direction of adjoining Zone
  Lots, the area which may be credited as outside area of window exposure,
  extends to required setback lines, regardles~ of the actual location of structures.
   (See Graphic Fig. No. 5.)                                                    r

                   .9-8.           SPECIAL ZONE LOT PLAN FOR PLANNED BUILDING

           .9-1.   DESCRIPTION OF DISTRICT.
                 This district is composed of certain land and structures used primarily
           to provide retail~g and personal se!'Vices -of all k~ds, including temporary
           housin,g, for non-residents of the distr.ict and transients using the main arterial
           highways along whi'ch the district is located, to" provide business services,
           such as 'warehousing and business machine· supply companies, in support of
           the primary activities in the central business district and to provide locations
           for certain limited industrial activities which require a higher degree of
           protection from external effects of uses than is ~fforded in the principal indu&-
           trial distric~s. As a secondary function, retailing and perso~al servjces may
           be provided to residents of ne8l'-by areas. Purchasing in the district is primarily
           by adults of both sexes, rarely accompanied by their children, and the volume
           of pedestrian traffic in proportion io automobile traffic is relatively low. This
           dh;trict is usually located 10 long narrow strips paralleling arterial liighways
           in the city or in 8l'eas adjacent to the central business_ district, characteristically
           is large, and almost always extensively aliuts residential districts. The regula-
           tions are designed ·to permit developmeµt of the enumerated functions, limited
           by standards desigD,!ld to protect the abutting or surrounding residentiaJ dis-
           tricts. To' these ends, the regulations establish standards -retaining as low an
           intensity of use and concentration of vehicles ~s is compatible with the function-
           ing of the district.               -

seventy decibels at any po.int of any boundary ,line of the. Zone J;.ot on which
the use is located.

recurrently generated is not ,perceptible, without instruments, at any poii)t of
any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located.

           .10-1.   DESCRIPTION OF DISTRICT.
                 This district is COIDp08ed of certain land and structures used primarily
           to provide retailing and personal and business services of all kinds satisfying
           the needs of transients generally and of the residents, business and industry
           of the entire metropolitan area and the outlying trade area, plus wholesaling
           and limited manufacturing in support of the prup.ary. functions. Purfhasing
           in the district is primarily by adults of both sexes, sometimes accompanied by
           their children, and the volume of pedestrian traffic in 'proportion ·to the volume
           of automobile and public transit traffic entering tl;te district is very low. This
           district is located at the area of convergence of ·th~ ~in arterial highways
           and transit lines, 1s large, is surrounded by non-residential districts' or ' }jy the
           R-4 District and is centrally located with respect to th~ entire metr<lpolitan
           area. The regulations are designed to permit a highly concentrated, intensive
           development of the enumerated facilities, -limited hy standards design~ to
           provide light and air for street exposures of buildings in , the distric! and to
           protect the district itself from over-intensive · development of Ikcl coverage
           and over-congestion as related to the ultimate capacity •of common :public
           facilities which serve the entire district. Because transit facilities are centered
           in this district and because the provision of o:ff-st;~t parking is a recognized,
           separate business enterprise, the regulations do ~ot :·~ire ~e furnis~g of
           off-street parking space.

           provisions become effective, every use shall be made l~ ~i:pply with the follow-

           .10-2 (1).   ENCLOSURE OF USES.
                Every use, unless expressly exempted by this orcfuiarire, shall be operated
           in its ~ntirety within a completely enclosed stru<;t~;, the exemption of a use
           from the requirement of enclosure will be indicated by the phrase "need not
           be enclosed" appearing after any use exempted.       ·     '


           .10-2 (2).   VOLUME OF SOUND GENERATED.
                Every use, unless expressly exempted by this ~rdinance, ,shall be so operated
           that the volume of sound inherently and recurrently generated does not e:iceed
           seventy decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone L>t on which
           the use is located.                                                         ·


           .10-2 (3).   VIBRATION GENERATED.

                Every use shall be so operated that the ground vibration inherently and
           recurrently generated is not perceptible, without instruments, at any point
           of any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located.

    .10-2 (4). EIIJssloN OF lk&T, G.u.RE, RADIATION AND FUMES.
         Every use shall be so operated that it does not emit an obnoxious or
    dangerous degne of heat,. glare; radiation or fumes beyond any boundary line
    of the Zone Lot on which.,_the use is located.

    . 10-2 (5).   OUTDOOR STORAGE AND WASTE DISPOSAL. ·

         .10-2 (5) (a). No highly flammable or explosive liquids, solids or gases
    shall be stored in hulk above ground. Tanks or drums of fuel directly connect-
    ing with heating devices or appliances located on the same Zone Lot as the
    tanks or drums of fuel are excluded from this provision;
        .10-2 (5) (h). All outdoor storage facilities for fuel. raw materials and
    products shall he enclosed by a fence or wall adequate to conceal such facilities
    from adjacent property;
         .10-2 (5) (c). No materials or wastes shall he deposited upon a Zone
    Lot in such form or manner that they may he transferred off the Zone Lot by
    natural causes or forces;
         .10-2 (5) (d). All materials or wastes which might cause fumes or
    dust or which constitute a fire hazard or which may he edible by or otherwise
    he attractive to rodents or insects shall he stored outdoors only in closed
    containers.

    .10-3. PERMITTED USE.5.
         No land shall he used or occupied and no structure shall he designed,
    erected, altered, used or occupied except for either one or several of the follow-
    ing Uses by Right and lawful Accessory Uses thereto or for one or several
    of the following Uses by Temporary Permit.

           .10-3 ( 3). ACCESSORY USES.
                Incidental only to a Use by Right, any use which complies with all of the
           following conditions may be operated, either in a detached structure or in the
           structure containing the Use by Right, as an Accessory Use:
                .10-3 (3) (a). Is clearly incidental and customary to and commonly
           associated with the operation of the Use by Right;
               .10-3 (3) (b). Is operated and maintained under the same ownership,
           or by lessees or concessionaires thereof, and oil the same Zone Lot as the Use
           by Right;
                .10-3 ( 3) ( c). Does not include structures or structural features incon-
           sistent with the Use by Right;
                .10-3 (3) (d). Does not include residential occupancy in conjunction
           with uses other than hotels, motels, tourist homes and like transient housing
           accommoMio• except by owners and employees employed on the premises
           and the intmediate families of such owners and employees;
                .10-3 ( 3) ( e). Does not utilize a gross floor area in excess of ten per
           cent of the gross floor area utilized by the Use by Right; provided, however,
                .10-3 (3) (f). That the gross floor area utilized by all Accessory Uses
           ( except garages, lading docks and company dining rooms) to all Uses by
           Right in the same structure shall not be in excess of ten per cent of the gross
           floor area utilized by all of the Uses by Right.

=== page 108 ===
                                                                                   53     141




.10-4.   PERMITTED STRUCTURES•
•10-4 (1). ZONE LOT FOR STRUCTURES.
      A ground area, herein called the Zone Lot, shall be designated, provided
and continuously maintained for all structures containimg a Use by Right.
Each Zone Lot shall have at least one front line and shall be occupied only by
one or more structures each containing a Use by Right and an equal number
of subordinate structures containing only Accessory Uses. Upon application
to and approval hy the Department of Zoning Administration, the boundaries
and area of a designated Zone Lot may he amended if full compliance with
all requirements of this ordinance can be maintained.

.10-4 (2). BULK OF STRUCTURES.
     No part of any structure or fixture thereof shall extend out over public
rights-of-way and no part of any structure (except church spires, church
towers, flagpoles, antennas, chimneys, flues, vents or accessory water tanks)
shall project up through hu1k limits which are defined hy planes starting at
the center lines of all streets within and abutting the district and extending up
over the Zone Lot at an angle of 75 degrees and 4 minutes with respect to the
horizontal ( a e_itch of 3. 75 feet additional rise for each foot additional seth.k )..
(See Graphic Fig. No. 3.)

.10-4 (3). MAXIMUM GROSS FLOOR AREA IN STRUCTURES.
     The sum total of the gross floor area of all structures on a Zone Lot,
excluding garage space, shall not he greater than ten times the area of the
Zone Lot on which the structures are located.
•10-4 (4). OUTSIDE AREA OF WINDOW ExPOSURE.
     Each legally required window shall have not less than the following amount
of outside exposure determined in the following manner: From a reference
point located at the bottom center of the window, extend outward at right
angle to the window plane, a horizontal sector of 140 degrees, centered on
the window, with a radius of ten feet. Within this sector the minimum re-
quired outside area of exposure for the window shall he any open sector or
combination of sectors totalling 70 degrees. Applied in the direction of ad-
joining Zone Lots, the area which may be credited as outside area of window
exposure, extends to required setback lines, regardless of the actual location
of structures. (See Graphic Fig. No. 5.)
.10-5. PERMITTED SIGNS . .
     The provisions of Article 613. Permitted Signs shall be of no force and
effect in this district; provided, however, that no sign, outdoor commercial
advertising device or lighting device constituting a nuisance to an adjacent
residential district, because of lighting glare, focus, animation or flashing
of the sign, lighting or advertising device, shall he erected or continued in
operation•
.10-6. OFF-STREET PARKING REQUIREMENTS.
     The provisioM of Article 614. Off-Street Parking Requirements shall he
of no force and effect in this district.
•10-7. OFF-STREET LOADING REQUIREMENTS.
     The provisions of Article 615. Off-Street Loading Requirements shall he
in full force and effect in this district.

           . 10-9 (4). REVIEW OF APPLICATION FOR APPROVAL.
                 All applications hereunder shall be reviewed by the Department of
           Zoning Administration and either approved or disapproved. Any approval
           hereunder may establish necessary conditions and limitations.
           .10-9 ( 5). APPROVED PLAN REGISTERED AND RECORDED.
                Upon approval of a special plan hereunder, a copy of such plan shall
           be registered among the records of the Department of Zoning Administration
           and a copy of such plan, or such other record thereof as deemed proper by
           the Department, shall be recorded by the Department in the office of the
           Clerk and Recorder .
           •10-9 (6). EFFECT OF REGISTERED AND REcoRDED SPECIAL PLANS.
                 All special plans registered and recorded hereunder shall be binding
           upon the applicants for such special plans, their successors and assigns, shall
           limit and control the issuance and validity of all zoning permits and zoning
           certificates and shall restrict and limit the use and operation of all land and
           structures included within such special plans to all conditions and limitations
           specified in such plans and the approvals thereof .
           .10-9 (7). AMENDMENT OF REGISTERED AND RECORDED SPECIAL PLANS.
                All special plans registered and recorded hereunder may be amended
           pursuant to the same procedure and subject to the same limitations and
           requirements by which such plans were approved, registered and recorded.
           .10-9 (8). WITHDRAWAL OF REGISTERED AND RECORDED SPECIAL PLANS.
                Upon application to the Department of Zoning Administration by the
           owner or owners of the entire land area included within any special plan

               .11-8.         SPECIAL ZONE LOT PLAN FOR PLANNED BUILDING
                              GROUPS.

           •11-1. DESCRIPTION OF DISTRICT.
                This district is composed of certain lands located between general indus-
           trial uses and quiet residential areas and of certain other lands so located
           that residential development is not feasible but where residential development
           is in close proximity. The regulations of this district are intended to provide
           structural standards, standards of intensity of use and standards of external
           effects compatible with the surrounding or abutting residential districts. To
           these ends, development is limited to a low concentration, external effects are
           limited and permitted uses are limited to those manufacturing and wholesaling
           activities which can be operated in a clean and quiet manner, plus certain

=== page 119 ===
                                                                                  53   151




public facilities which are needed to serve the occupu.ts of the district and
residents of adjoining residential districts.      ·
.11-2. LIMITATIONS ON EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES.
     Within not more than three years from the date on wliich the following
provisions become effective, every use shall be made to comply with the
following Limitations:
.11-2 (l). ENCLOSURE OF USES.
     Every use, unless expressly exempted by this ordina:nce, shall be operated
in its entirety within a completely enclosed structure; the exemption of a use
from the requirement of enclosure will be indicated by the phrase "need not
be enclosed" appearing after any use exempted .
.11-2 (2). VOLUME OF SOUND GENERATED.
     Every use, unless expressly exempted by this ordinance, shall be so
operated that the volume of sound inherently and recurrently generated does
not exceed sixty decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone Lot
on which the use is located .
.11-2 (3). VIBRATION GENERATED.
     Every use shall be so operated that the ground vibration inherently and
recurrently generated is not perceptible, without instruments, at any point
of any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located.
.11-2 (4). EMISSION OF HEAT, GLARE, RADIATION AND FUMES.
     Every use shall be so operated that it does not emit an obnoxious or
dangerous degree of heat, glare, radiation or fumes beyond any boundary line
of the Zone Lot on which the use is located.
•11-2 (5).   OUTDOOR STORAGE AND WASTE DISPOSAL•

     .11-2 (5) (a). No highly fianunable or explosive liquids, solids or
gases shall be stored in bulk above ground. Tanks or drums of fuel directly
connecting with heating devices or appliances located on the same Zone Lot
as the tanks or drums of fuel are excluded from this provision;

      .11-2 (5) (b). All outdoor storage facilities for fuel, raw materials
and products shall be enclosed by a fence or wall adequate to conceal such
facilities from adjacent property;
    .11-2 (5) (c). No materials or wastes shall be deposited upon a Zone
Lot in such form or manner that they may be transferred off the Zone Lot
by natural causes or forces;
     .11-2 (5) (d). All materials or wastes which might cause fumes or
dust or which constitute a fire hazard or which may be edible by or otherwise
be attractive to rodents or insects shall be stored outdoors only in closed
containers.
.11-3. PERMITTED l,JSE~. .,
     No land shall be used or oct:apied and no structure shall be designed.
erected, altered, used or occupied except £« either one or several of the follow-
ing Uses by Right and lawful Accessory Uses thereto or for one or several of
the following Uses by Temporary Permit. The number enclosed in parentheses
which follows any use shall not be deemed a limitation of the use but shall be
deemed the classification of the use for the purposes of off-street parking
requirements.

           •11-3 (3). ACCESSORY USES.
                Incidental only to a Use by Right, any use which complies with all of the
           following conditions may he operated as an Accessory Use:
                .11-3 (3) {a). Is clearly incidental and customary to and commonly •
           associated with the operation of the Use by Right;
               .11-3 (3) (h). Is operated and maintained under the same ownership,
           or by lessees or concessionaires thereof, and on the same Zone Lot as the Use
           by Right;
                .11-3 {3) {c). Does not include structures or structural features incon-
           sistent with the Use by Right;
                .11-3 {3) ( d). Does not include residential occupancy except by care-
           takers or watchmen;
                .11-3 (3) {e). If operated partially or entirely in detached structures,
           such detached structures shall he limited to a gross floor area of not more
           than ten per cent of the area of the Zone Lot on which the Use by Right is
           located;
                .11-3 (3) {f). If operated partially or entirely within the structure con-
           taining the Use by Right, the gross floor area within such structure utilized by
           Accessory Uses (except garages, loading docks and company dining rooms}
           shall not be greater than ten per cent of the gross floor area of the structure
           containing the Use by Right .
           •11-4.   PERMITTED STRUCTURES .
           .11-4 (1). ZONE LOT FOR STRUCTURES.
                A specific ground area, herein called the Zone Lot, shall he designated,
           provided and continuously maintained for all structures containing a Use or
           Uses by Right. Each Zone Lot shall have at least one front line. Upon appli-
           cation to and approval by the Department of Zoning Administration, the
           houndariei and area of a designated Zone Lot may be amended if full compli-
           ance with all requirements of this ordinance can he maintained .
           .11-4 (2). LOCATION OF STRUCTURES.
                Except for Permitted Encroachments and for use as open off-street parking
           space for automobiles, the space resulting from the following setbacks shall
           he open and unobstructed.

       .11-4 (2) (a). Front Setback. All structures shall be set in a distance of

  feet for all other structures.

  and :fixtures for the disposal of trash or garbage; and provided, further, that
  if a Zone Lot has more than one rear line by reason of abutting alleys, the
  aforesaid setbacks shall be required from only one rear line and the other
  rear line or lines shall, for the purposes of setbacks, be deemed side lines of
  the Zone Lot.

                          Each legally required window shall have not less than the following
                    amount of outside exposure determined in the following manner: From a
                    reference point located at the bottom center of the window, extend outward at
                    right angle to the window plane, a horizontal sector of 140 degrees, centered
                    on the window, with a radius of 10 feet. Within this sector the minimum
                    required outside area of exposure for the window shall be any open sector or
           ....     combination of sectors totalling 70 degrees. Applied in the direction of ad-
                     joining Zone Lots, the area which may be credited as outside area of window
                    exposure, extends to required setback lines, regardless of the actual location
                    of structures. (See Graphic Fig. No. 5)

.12-1. DESCRIPTION OF DISTRICT.
      This district is composed of certain lands so situated as to be suitable for
industrial development, but where the modes of operations of the industry will
directly affect nearby residential and business uses. The purpose of the district
is to permit the normal operations of almost all industries, subject only to those
regulations needed to control congestion and to protect nearby residential and
business districts.
.12-2. LIMITATIONS ON EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES.
     Within not more than three years from the date on which the following
provisions become effective, every use shall he made to comply with the follow-
ing Limitations:
.12-2 (1). VOLUME OF SOUND GENERATED.
     Every use, unless expressly exempted by this ordinance, shall be so
operated that the vomme of sound inherently and recurrently generated does
not exceed seventy decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone
Lot on which the use is located.
.12-2 (2). VIBI.UTION GENERATED.
     Every use shall be so operated that the ground vibration inherently and
recurrently generated is not perceptible, without instruments, at any point of
any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located.

=== page 126 ===
53   158




           .12-2 (3).       EMISSION oF HEAT, GLARE, RADIAnoN AND Fu11U.                ...
                Every use shall be so operated that it does not emit an obnoxioas or
           dangerous degree of heat, glare, rad~ or fumes beyond any boundary line
           of the-Zone Lot on which the use is located.
           . 12-2 (4). OUTDOOR STORAGE AND WASTE DISPOSAL•
                 .12-2 (4) (a). No highly flammable or explosive liquids, solids or gases
           shall be stored in bulk above ground. Tanks or drums of fuel for railroad loco-
           motive fueling or directly connecting with energy devices, heating devices or
           appliances located on the same Zone Lot as the tanks or drums of fuel are
           excluded from this provision;
                .12-2 (4) (b). All outdoor storage facilities for fuel, raw materials and
           products and all fuel, raw materials and products stored outdoors shall be
           enclosed by a solid fence or wall adequate to conceal such facilities, fuel, raw
           materials and products from adjacent residential and business districts; pro-
           vided, however, that such fence or wall need not exceed ten feet in height;
                .12-2 (4) (c). No materials or wastes shall be deposited upon a Zone
           Lot in such form or manner that they may be transferred off the Zone Lot by
           natural causes or forces;
                .12-2 (4) (d). All materials or wastes which might cause fumes or
           dust or which constitute a fire hazard or which may be edible by or o_therwise
           be attractive to rodents or insects shall be stored outdoors only in closed
           containers.
           .12-3. PERMITTED USES.
                No land shall be used or occupied and no structure shall be designed,
           erected, altered, used or occupied except for either one or several of the follow-
           ing Uses by Right and lawful Accessory Uses thereto or for one or several of
           the following Uses by Temporary Permit. The number enclosed in parentheses
           which follows any use shall not be deemed a limitation of the use but shall be
           deemed the classification of the use for the purposes of off-street parking
           requirements.

               .12-3 (3) (b). Is operated and maintained under the same ownership,
           or by lessees or concessionaires thereof, and on the same Zone Lot as the
           Use by Right;
                .12-3 (3) (c). Does not include structures or structural features incon-
           sistent with the Use by Right;
                .12-3 (3) (d). Does not include residential occupancy except by care-
           takers or watchmen;
                .12-3 (3) {e). If operated partially or entirely in detached structures,
           such detached structures shall be limited to a gross floor area of not more than
           ten per cent of the area of the Zone Lot on which the Use by Right is located;

     J2-3 (3) (£). If operated partially or entirely within the structure
C8111:aining the Use by Right, the gross floor area within such structure utilized
by Accessory Uses (except garages, loading docks and company dining rooms)
shall not be greater than ten per cent of the gross floor area of the structure.
.12-4. PERMITTED STRUCTURES .
.12-4 (1). ZONE LOT FOR STRUCTURES.
     A specific ground area, herein called the Zone Lot, shall be designated,
provided and continuously maintained for all structures containing a Use or
Uses by Right. Each Zone Lot shall have at least one front line. Upon applica-
tion to and approval by the Department of Zoning Administration, the
boundaries and area of a designated Zone Lot may be amended if full compli-
ance with all requirements of this ordinance can be maintained.
•12-4 (2). LOCATION OF STRUCTURES.
     All structures in which petroleum is refined shall be set in a distance of
at least one hundred fifty feet from each boundary line of the Zone Lot on
which such structures are located .
.12-4 (3). MAxlMUM GROSS FLOOR AREA IN STRUCTURES.
     The sum total of the gross floor area of all structures on a Zone Lot,
excluding the gross floor area of off-street parking garages, shall not be greater
than two times the area of the Zone Lot on which the structures are located.
.12-4 (4). OUTSIDE AREA OF WINDOW EXPOSURE.
     Each legally required window shall have not less than the following
amount of outside exposure determined in the following manner: From a refer-
ence point located at the bottom center of the window, extend outward at right
angle to the window plane, a horizontal sector of 140 degrees, centered on the
window, with a radius of IO feet. Within this sector the minimum required
outside area of exposure for the window shall be any open sector or combina•
tion of sectors totalling 70 degrees. Applied in the direction of adjoining Zone
Lots, the area which may be credited as outside area of window exposure,
extends to required setback lines, regardless of the actual location of structures.
 (See Graphic Fig. No. 5.)
.12-5. PERMITTED SIGNS.
     The provisions of Article 613. Permitted Signs shall be of no force and
effect in this district; provided, however, that no sign, outdoor commercial
advertising device or lighting device constituting a nuisance to an adjacent
residential district, because of lighting glare, focus, animation or flashing of
the sign, lighting or advertising device, shall be erected or continued in
operation .
. 12-6. OFF-STREET PARKING REQUIREMENTS.
      The provisions of Article 614. Off-Street Parking Requirements shall be
in full force and effect in this district.
•12-7. OFF-STREET LOADING REQUIREMENTS.
     The provisions of Article 615. Off-Street Loading Requirements shall be
in full force and effect in this district.
. 12-8. SPECIAL ZONE LOT PLAN FOR PLANNED BUILDING GROUPS.
      The provisions of Article 616. Special Zone Lot Plan for Planned Building
Groups shall be of no force and effect in this district.

     •13-1. DESCRIPTION OF DISTRICT.
          This district is composed primarily of lands located deep within the
     industrial areas of the city and down-wind from residential and business areas.
     Regulations are the minimum required for mutual protection of the industrial
     users, and to that end, the district should not be adjacent to any residential
     or business district, if such abutment can possibly be avoided•
     .13-2. LIMITATIONS ON EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES.
          Within not more than three years· from the date on which the following
     provisions become effective, every use shall be made to comply with the follow-
     ing Limitations:
     .13-2 (1). EMISSION OF HEAT, GLARE, RADIATION AND FUMES.
          Every use shall be so operated that it does not emit a dangerous degree
     of heat, glare, radiation or fumes beyond any boundary line of the Zone Lot
     on which the use is located,
     .13-2 (2). OUTDOOR STORAGE AND WASTE DISPOSAL•
          .13-2 (2) (a). No materials or wastes shall be deposited upon a Zone
     Lot in such form or manner tliat they may be transferred off the Zone Lot by
     natural causes or forces;
          .13-2 (2) (b). All materials or wastes which might cause fumes or
     dust or which constitute a fire hazard or which may be edible by or otherwise
     be attractive to rodents or insects shall be stored outdoors only in closed
     containers;

.14-1. DESCRIPTION OF DISTRICT.
     This district is composed of certain large open land areas. The regulations
of this district are designed to retain the open character of this land, and to that
end, the uses are limited to open recreational uses, airports and agricultural
activities.

.15-1. DESCRIPTION OF DISTRICT.
     This district is composed of certain lands used for off-street parking where
they abut a business or industrial district, and are surrounded on at least two
sides by residence areas. The district regulations are designed to permit off-street
parking facilities to be established to support business and industrial uses, with-
out permitting the business or industrial uses per se to extend into residential
areas, and to reduce congestion in the streets caused by certain uses with a mini-
mum of incompatibility with abutting residential areas. To these ends, the uses
are limited solely to parking and a continuation of existing railroad rights-of-way•

•15-2. PERMITTED USES.
     No land shall be used or occupied and no structure shall be designed,
erected, altered, used or occupied except for one only of the following Uses by
Right.

.15-2 (1). USES BY RIGHT.
     The following uses may be operated as Uses by Right:
     .15-2 ( 1) (a). Parking lot: A parking lot operated and maintained in
accordance with all of the following conditions:
    .15-2 (I) (a) (a-1). Is not used for the sale, repair or dismantling of
any vehicles, equipment, materials or supplies;
    .15-2 (1) (a) (a-2). Is properly graded for drainage; surfaced with
concrete, asphahic concrete, asphalt, oil or any other dust-free surfacing and
maintained in good condition, free of weeds, dust, trash or debris;
     .15-2 (I) (a) ( a-3) . Is provided with entrances and exits so located
as to minimize traffic congestion and the effect of headlights at night;

           . 15-3. PERMITTED STRUCTURES.
           .15-3 ( l). ZONE LOT FOR STRUCTURES.
                 A separate ground area, herein called the Zone Lot, shall be designated,
           provided and continuously maintained for each structure containing a Use by
           Right. Each Zone Lot shall have at least one front line and shall be occupied
           only by the structure containing the Use by Right. Upon application to and
           approval by the Department of Zoning Administration, the boundaries and area
           of a designated Zone Lot may be amended if full compliance with all requirements
           of this ordinance can be maintained.
           .15-3 (2). LoCATION OF STRUCTURES.
                 The space resulting from the following setbacks shall be open and un-
           obstructed.
                 . 15-3 (2) (a) . Front Setback. All structures shall be set in a distance of
           not less than twenty feet from each front line of the Zone Lot; provided, however,
           that on the two shorter dimensions of a block oblong in shape, the front setback
           may be reduced to ten feet .
                  .15-3 (2) (b). Rear Setback. If no alley abuts the rear line of the Zone
           Lot, all structures shall be set in a distance of not less than twenty feet from each
           rear line of the Zone Lot. If an alley abuts the rear line of the Zone Lot, all
           structures shall be set in a distance of not less than twenty feet from the center
           line of the abutting alley; provided, however, that if a Zone Lot has more than
           one rear line by reason of abutting alleys, the aforesaid setback shall be re-
           quired from only one rear line and the other rear line or lines shall, for the pur-
           poses of setbacks, be deemed side lines of the Zone Lot.

shall be set in a distance of not less than five feet from each side line of the

 .15-3 ( 3) . BULK OF STRUCTURES.
      No part of any structure (except flagpoles, antennas, chimneys flaes, vents
 or acceasory water tanks) shall project up through bulk limits whici: e"e de-
 fined by planes starting at horizontal lines which are co-directional to the front,
 rear and side lines of the Zone Lot and pass through points ten feet above the
 midpoint of each such front, rear and side line and which planes extend up over
 the Zone Lot at an angle of forty-fiTe degrees with respect to the horizontal (a
 pitch of one foot additional rise for each foot additional setback). {See Graphic
 Fig. No. 1)
 .15-3 (4). OUTSIDE AREA OF WINDOW ExPOSURE.
       Each legally required window shall have not less than the following amount
 of outside exposure determined in the following manner: From a reference point
 located at the bottom center of the window, extend outward at right angle to the
 window plane, a horizontal sector of 140 degrees, centered on the window, with
 a radius of 10 feet. Within this sector the minimum required outside area of
 exposure for the window shall be any open sector or combination of sectors
 totalling 70 degrees. Applied in the direction of adjoining Zone Lots, the area
 which may be credited as outside area of window exposure, extends to required
 setback lines, regardless of the actual location of structures. (See Graphic Fig.
 No. 5) '
 .15-4. PERMITTED SIGNS.
      The provisions of Article 613. Permitted Signs shall be in full force and
 effect in this district.
 . 15-5. OFF-STREET PARKING REQUIREMENTS.
       The provisions of Article 614. Off-Street Parking Requirements shall be of
 no force and effect in this district.
 .15-6. OFF-STREET LOADING REQUIREMENTS.
      The provisions of Article 615. Off-Street Loading Requirements shall be
 of no force and effect in this district.
 . 15-7. SPECIAL ZONE LOT PLAN FOR PLANNED BUILDING GROUPS.
       The provisions of Article 616. Special Zone Lot Plan for Planned Building
 Groups shall be of no force and effect in this district.

           from the requirement of enclosure will be indicated by the phrase "need not be
            enclosed" appearing after any use exempted.

.16-2 ( 5) . OUTDOOR STORAGE AND WASTE DISPOSAL•
     .16-2 (5) (a). No highly flammable or explosive liquids, solids or gases
shall be stored in bulk above ground. Tanks or drums of fuel directly connecting
with heating devices or appliances located on the same Zone Lot as the tanks or
drums of fuel are excluded from this provision;
    .16-2 ( 5) (b). All outdoor storage facilities for fuel, raw materials and

in such form or manner that they may be transferred off the Zone Lot by natural
causes or forces;
      .16-2 ( 5) ( d). All materials or wastes which might cause fumes or dust
or which constitute a fire hazard or which may be edible by or otherwise be
attractive to rodents or insects shall be stored outdoors only in closed containers.

           All structures shall be located in accordance with and without any deviation
       from the Site Plan adopted and approved, incorporated herein and made a part
       hereof.
       .16-4 (2). HEIGHT OF STRUCTURES.
             No building or structure designed for human occupancy shall exceed a
       height of fifteen stories and no other building or structure shall exceed a height
       of fifty feet .
       .16-4 (3).   MAx!MUM GROSS FLOOR AREA IN STRUCTURES.
            The sum total of the gross floor area of any bulding or buildings designed
       for human occupancy shall not exceed fifty per cent of the area of the land and
       the sum total of the gross floor area of all buildings shall not exceed twenty-five
       per cent of the area of land.
       .16-4 (4). DENSITY OF RESIDENTIAL DEVELOPMENT.
            The density of residential development shall not exceed one dwelling unit,
       with or without kitchen facilities, for each three thousand square feet of land.
       .16-4 (5).   OUTSIDE AREA OF WINDOW EXPOSURE.
            Each legally required wndow shall have not less than the following amount
       of outside exposure determined in the following manner: From a reference point
       located at the bottom center of the window, extend outward at right angle to the
       window plane, a horizontal sector of 140 degrees, centered on the window, with
       a radius of 10 feet. Within this sector the minimum required outside area of
       exposure for the window shall be any open sector or combination of sectors
       totalling 70 degrees. Applied in the direction of adjoining Zone Lots, the area
       which may be credited as outside area of window exposure extends to required
       setback lines regardless of the actual location of structures. (See Graphic Fig.
       No. 5)
       .16-5.   PERMITTED SIGNS.
            The provisions of Article 613. Permitted Signs shall be in full force and
       effect in this district.

           dwelling or the dwelling unit in a multiple unit dwelling and only by the person
           or persons maintaining a dwelling therein;
                .17-3 (4) (b) (b-2).    Does not have a separate entrance from outside
           the building;
                .17-3 (4) (b) (b-3). Does not display or create outside the building any
           external evidence of the operation of the Home Occupation except, for each
           street front of the Zone Lot on which the building is located, one unanimated,
           non-illuminated flat or window sign having an area of not more than one hun-
           dred square inches;
                .17-3 (4) (b) (b-4). Does not utilize more than twenty per cent of the
           gross floor area, but not to exceed three hundred square feet in the single unit
           dwelling or the dwelling unit in the multiple unit dwelling; provided, however,
           that this limitation shall not apply to foster family care;
                .17-3 (4) (b) (b-5). Does not have any employees or regular assistants
           not residing in the single unit dwelling or the dwelling unit in the multiple unit
           dwelling;
                .17-3 (4) (b) (b-6). Is limited to the use of electric motors for power,
           with a total limitation of three horsepower.

                                     613.
                                Permitted Signs
    613.1.     SCOPE OF REGULATIONS .
       .2.     GENERAL LIMITATIONS.
       .3.     CLASSIFICATION OF SIGNS .
       •4.     PERMITTED USE AND LOCATION OF SIGNS .
       •5.     DETERMINATION OF SIZE OF SIGNS .
               .5-1. Flat, Wall and Window Signs.                                          •
    613.1.       Scope of Reg ulations.
          The regulations herein set forth shall apply and govern in all districts ex-
    cept the B-4, B-5, 1-l and 1-2 Districts in which districts these regulations shall
    be of no force and effect; provided, however, that in the enumerated excluded
    districts no sign, outdoor commercial advertising device or Jjghting device con•
    stituting a nuisance to an adjacent residential district, because of lighting glare,
    focus, animation or flashing of the sign, lighting or advertising device, shall be
    erected or continued in operation.

    6 13 .2. Genera l Li mi ta tions.
         No sign or class or classes of signs shall be erected or maintained unless the
    sign or class or classes of signs complies with all of the following conditions:
    .2-1.
         Is erected and maintained only by a Use by Right specifically enumer-
    ated for the district in which the sign is located and which Use by Right is a
    primary use of the premises on which the sign is located;
    .2-2.
            Is clearly incidental, customary to and commonly associated with the opera-
    tio.JJ. of the Use by Right;
    .2-3.
        Is established and controlled under and by the same ownership as the Use
    by Right;
    .2-4.
            Is limited in location to the premises on which the Use by Right is located;
    .2-5.
         Is limited in height and horizontal projection by the same bulk control
    planes governing all other structures in the district in which the sign is located
    and is further limited in particular size or height by splrifications in the re-
    spective and applicable sign class;
    .2-6.
         Is limited in subject matter to the name, design, picture or trademark
    of the owner, operator, builder, sales agent, managing agent, lessor or lessee of
    the premises or of the activities (including merchandise handled or services
    rendered) on the premises on which such sign is located and does not include

=== page 162 ===
53   192




           any general commercial advertising unrelated to or extending in substantial
           degree beyond the enumerated permitted subjects;                                        _)
           .2-7.
                   Planes of sign faces of two or more signs to be allocated to different street   -.
           fronts shall be not less than forty-five degrees from a continuous plane and such
           signs shall be separted by not less than ten feet.

           613.3. Classification of Signs.
                The following classification of signs shall be deemed to include all signs
           permitted in any district. If for any reason the classification of any sign is not
           readily determinable, the classification of such sign shall be fixed by the Depart-
           ment of Zoning Administration .
           .3-1.  CLASS 1.

           Class l:

                Maximum Size of Single Sign: One hundred square inches;
                Maximum Height Above Grade at Sign: Attached to building;
                Limitations on Number of Signs or Total Area of Signs: One sign for
           each street front of the Zone Lot on which the Use bv Right is located;
                Other Limitations or Conditions: Shall be neither illuminated nor animated.
           •3-2.   CLASS 2.
                The following signs meeting the following specifications shall constitute
           Class 2:
                Structural Type: Flat, wall or window sign; single face only;
                Maximum Size of Single Sign: Twelve square feet;
                Maximum Height Above Grade at Sign: Attached to building;
                Limitations on Number of Signs or Total Area of Signs: One sign for each
           street front of the Zone Lot on which the Use by Right is located;
                Other Limitations or Conditions: Shall be neither flashing nor animated;
           may be illuminated, but only from a concealed light source, until 11 :00 p.m.
           or any later hour required by law.
           •3-3.  CLASS 3.
                The following signs meeting the following specifications shall constitute
           Oass 3:
                Structural Type: Flat, wall or window sign; single face only;
                Maximum Size of Single Sign: Twenty square feet;
                Maximum Height Above Grade at Sign: Attached to building;
                Limitations on Number of Signs or Total Area of Signs: One sign for each
           street front of the Zone Lot on which the Use by Right is located;
                Other Limitations or Conditions: Shall be neither flashing nor animated;
           may be illuminated, but only from a concealed light source, until 11 :00 p.m.
           or any later hour required by law.

                The following signs meeting the following specifications shall constitute
           Class 4:

or any later hour required by law.

613.5.      Determination of Size of Signs .
.5-1. FLAT, WALL AND WINDOW SIGNS.
     The size of flat, wall and window signs shall he measured and determined in
the following manner:
.5-1 (I).
     When such signs are on a plate or framed, all of the plate or frame shall
he included in the dimensions;
.5-1 (2)
     When such signs are not on a plate or framed hut are partly or entirely
outlined hy a light line or area, or if on a plate or frame and circumscribed by a
larger light line or area, all of the area circumscribed by a light line or area
shall be included in the dimension;
.5-1 (3).
     When such signs consist only of letters, designs or figures engraved, painted,
projected or fixed on a wall, or free-standing within six inches of a wall, the
total area of the signs shall be the area of the smallest rectangle or circle within
which all of the fixed lettering may he inscribed.

614.3.    Location of Off-Street f0ir~ SJ,ffl:e.
     Off-street parking space shall be located on die 88lile Zone FAt • the 111e
for which provided and may he composed of one or sevetal separated parcea.

614.4.    Separate or Combined Space.
     Separate off-street parking space shall he provided for each use or the park-
ing space required of two or more uses located on the same Zone Lot may be com-
bined and used jointly; provided, however;
.4-1.
     Where off-street parking space is combined and used jointly by two or
more uses having different standards for determining the amount of off-street
parking space required, the parking space shall he adequate in area to provide
the sum total of off-street parking space requirements of all such uses;
                                                               '
.4-2.
                                                               '
     Where off-street parking space is combined and used jointly by two or
more uses having the same standard for determining the amount of off-street
parking space required, all of such uses, for the purposes of this section, shall he
considered a single unit and the gross floor area of all such uses in all structures
on the same Zone Lot or the number of employees of all such uses in all structures
on the same Zone Lot, as fixed by the applicable standard, shall be taken as a
single total for the purpose of determining the amount of off-street parking space
required.

614.5.    Maintenance of Parking Space.
     Off-street parking space shall be maintained in accordance with the follow-
ing specifications:
.&-1.
     In the R-0, R-1, R-2, R-3 and R-4 Districts, shall be used only by vehicles
up to three-fourths ton manufacturer's capacity rating and having wheels not
to exceed seventeen inches ;
.5-2.
     Shall not be used for the salte, repair, dismantling or servicing of any
vehicles, equipment, materials or supplies;
.5-3.
     Shall he properly graded for drainage, surfaced with concrete, asphaltic
con,crete, asphalt, oil or any other dust-free surfacing and maintained in good
condition, free of weeds, dust, trash and debris;

    53   200

                      Shall be provided with barriers of such dimensions that occupants of adja-
                 cent strrictrues are not unreasonably disturbed, either by day or by night, by the
                 movetnent of vehicles.
                 .5-5.
                     Shall be provided with entrances and exits so locate«f as to min-fmize traffic
         r       congestion;
         ..._,   .5-6.
                      Shall be provided with wheel guards or bumper guards so located that no
                 part of parked vehicles will extend beyond the parking space;
                 .5-7.
                      Lighting facilities shall be so arranged that they neither unreasonably
                 disturb occupants of adjacent residential properties nor interfere with traffic;
                 .5-8
                       May have not more than one attendant shelter building conforming to
                 all setback requirements for structures in the district and which shelter building

       a copy of such plan, or such other record thereof as deemfld        ).y the De-
       partment, shall be recorded by the Department in the offit:~ of t e lClerk and
       Recorder.

       .7-6. EFFECT OF REGISTERED AND RECORDED SPECIAL PLANS.
            All special plans registered and recorded hereunder shall be binding upon
       the applicants for such special plans, their successors and assigns, shall limit and
       control the issuance and validity of all zoning permits and zoning certificates and
       shall restrict and limit the use and operation of all land and structures included
       within such special plans to all conditions and limitations specified in such plans
       and the approvals thereof.

       .7-7. AMENDMENT OF REGISTERED AND RECORDED SPECIAL PLANS.
           All special plans registered and recorded hereunder may be amended pur•
       suant to the same procedure and subject to the same limitations and requirements
       by which such plans were approved, registered and rec~rded.
       .7-8. WITHDRAWAL OF REGISTERED AND RECORDED SPECIAL PLANS.

                         615.
          Off-Street l:oacling Requirements

615.1.    SCOPE OF REGULATIONS .
    .2.   DUTY TO PROVIDE OFF-STREET LOADING SPACE.
   .3. LOCATION OF OFF-STREET LOADING SPACE.
    .4.   AMOUNT OF OFF-STREET LOADING SPACE REQUIRED.


615.1. Scope of Regulations.
     The regulations herein set forth shall apply and govern in all districts except
the P-1 District in which district these regulations shall be of no force and effect;
provided, however, that in the R-O, R-1, R-2, R-3 and R-4 Districts these regula-
tions shall apply and govern only those structures in which are operated a use or
uses, which use or uses, pursuant to the provisions of Section 614. hereof (Off.
Street Parking Requirements), are required to provide and maintain six hundred
and forty or more square feet of off-street parking space.

615.2.     Duty to Provide Off-Street Loading Space.
      The duty to provide the off-street loading space herein required shall be the
joint and several responsibility of the operator and owner of the structure or struc-
tures for which off-street loading space is required to be provided. No structure
shall be designed, erected, altered, used or occupied unless the off-street loading
space herein required is provided in at least the amount herein set forth; pro-
vided, however, that off-street loading space need not be provided for structures
actually used, occupied and operated on the effective date of this ordinance unless,
after the effective date of this ordinance, such structures are enlarged or ex-
panded, or the uses within such structures are enlarged, expanded or changed,
so as to require a greater amount of off-street loading space than the amount of
off-street loading space not required to be furnished by reason of the foregoing
exclusion, in which event, the structures hereby excluded shall not be used, oc-
cupied or operated unless there is provided for the increment only of such struc-
tures, and maintained as herein required, at least the amount of off-street load-
ing space that would be required hereunder if the increment were a separate
structure.

615.3.     Location of Off-Street Loading Space.
     Off-street loading space shall be located on the same Zone Lot as the struc-
ture for which provided and shall be composed of one parcel.

615.4. Amount of Off-Street Loading Space Required.
     At least the following amounts of off-street loading space shall be provided,
plus an area or means adequate for ingress and egress:

                         616.
          Special Zone Lot Plan for Planned
                   Building Groups
616.1.    PURPOSE OF PROCEDURE.
    .2.   LIMITATIONS ON PROCEDURE.
          .2-1. As To Scope.
          .2-2. As To Districts .
    .3.   APPLICATIONS FOR APPROVAL. HOW MADE AND CONTENTS•
    .4.   REVIEW OF APPLICATIONS FOR APPROVAL. STANDARDS .
          .4-1. Standard Provisions of Ordinance .
          .4-2. Site Facilities.
          .4-3. Minimum Spacing Between Buildings, Orientation
                of Main Window Exposures and Entrances.
    .5.   APPROVED SPECIAL PLANS REGISTERED AND RECORDED .
    .6.   EFFECT OF REGISTERED AND RECORDED SPECIAL PLANS .
    .7.   AMENDMENT OR WITHDRAWAL OF REGISTERED AND
          RECORDED SPECIAL PLANS.


616.1.     Purpose of Procedure.

      Under the standard provisions of this ordinance a separate ground area, re-
ferred to in the ordinance as the Zone Lot, must be designated, provided and con-
tinuously maintained for .each structure containing a Use or Uses by Right. Pur-
suant to the procedure hereinafter set forth, two or more of such structures may
be erected and maintained on the same Zone Lot. Also, several Zone Lots may be
combined into one special plan covering a planned building group. The procedure
is intended to perm.it diversification in the location of structures and to improve
circulation facilities and other site qualities while insuring adequate standards
relating to public health, safety, welfare and convenience in the use and occupan-
cy of buildings and facilities in planned building groups.

616.2.     Limitations on Procedure .

.2-1.   AS TO SCOPE.
     The procedure hereinafter set forth shall not be construed to waive nor shall
there he waived thereby any regulation for any district except the regulation that
a sepll?IIR ground area, herein called the Zone Lot, shall be designated, provided
and continuously maintained for each structure containing a Use or Uses by
Right.
.2-2. AS TO DISTRICTS.
     The procedure hereinafter set forth shall apply only in R-2, R-3, R-4, B-1,
B-2, B-3, B-4 and 0-1 Districts and in the 0-1 District shall apply only to build-
ings open to the public.

                                                                                                    'I

           616.3.       Application for Approval. How Made and Contents.
                All applications for approval of a special plan hereunder shall be filed with
           the Department of Zoning Administration by the owner or owners of the entire
           land area to he included within the special plan, the owner or owners of all struc-
           tures then existing on said land area and all incumbrancers of said land area and
           structures; shall contain sufficient evi-0.ence to establish that the applicants are
           the owners and all the incumbrancers of the designated land and structures; shall
           contain such information and representations required by this ordinance or
           deemed necessary by the Department and shall include plats and plans showing
           at least the following details drawn to scale:
           .3-1.
                 The land area which would be included within the special plan, the present
           zoning classification of the designated area, the land area of all abutting districts
           and the present zoning classification thereof, all public and private rights-of-way
           and easements bounding and intersecting the designated area and the abutting
           districts which are proposed to be continued, created, relocated and/or
           abandoned;
           .3-2.
                The proposed finished grade of the designated area, shown in contour
           intervals of not to exceed two feet;
           .3-3.
                A description of the proposed Zone Lot or Zone Lots and the boundaries
           thereof;
           .3-4
                The location of each existing and each proposed structure in the designated
           area, the use or uses to be contained therein, the number of stories, gross floor
           area and approximate location of entrances and loading points thereof;
           .3-5.
                   The location of all outside facilities for waste disposal;
           .3-6.
                All curb cuts, driving lanes, parking areas, loading areas, public trans-
           portation points and illumination facilities for the same;
           .3-7.
                All pedestrian walks, malls and open areas for use by tenants or members
           of the public;
           .3-8.
                   The location and height of all walls, fences and screen planting;
           .3-9.
               The location, size, height and orientation of all signs other than signs flat
           on building facades;
           .3-10.
                The types of surfacing, such as paving, turfing or gravel, to be used at the
           various locations;
           .3-11.
                The location of fire hydrants.                                                     -.

    616.4.    Review of Applications for Approval. Standards.
         All applications hereunder shall be reviewed for completeness by the Depart-
    ment of Zoning Administration and, if found to be complete, shall be transmitted
    to the Planning Office and to any other agency, either public or private, which
    might be affected by approvals of such applicati~ Any such agency, including

-   the Department of Zoning Administration, may transmit comments and recom-
    mendations to the Planning Office. All applications hereunder shall be reviewed
    by the Planning Office and approved or ,disapproved. Any approval hereunder
    may establish necessary conditions and limitations. All reviews hereunder shall
    be based up0n the following standards:
    .4-1. STANDARD PROVISIONS OF ORDINANCE.
          No application hereunder shall be approved unless the application and the
    accompanying plats and plans comply with all regulations established for the dis-
    trict or districts in which are located the land area and structures designated in
    such application, except the regulation that a separate ground area, herein called
    the Zone Lot, shall be designated, provided and continuously maintained for
    each structure containing a Use or Uses by Right.
    .4-2. SITE FACILITIES.
         All special plans hereunder shall make due provision for:
    .4-2 (I).
         Adequate design of grades, paving, gutters, drainage and treatment of tud
    to handle storm waters, prevent erosion and formation of dust;
    .4-2 (2).
          Adequate, safe and convenient arrangement of pedestrian circulation facil-
    ities, roadways, driveways, off-street parking and loading space, facilities for
    waste disposal and illumination;
    .4-2 (3).
         Adequate amount and proper location of pedestrian walks, malls and land-
    scaped spaces to prevent pedestrian use of vehicular ways and parking spaces
    and to separate pedestrian walks, malls and public transportation loading places
    from general vehicular circulation facilities;
    .4-2 (4).
          Arrangement of buildings and vehicular circulation open spaces so that
    pedestrians moving between buildings are not unnecessarily exposed to vehicular
    traffic;
    .4-2 (5).
         Proper arrangement of signs and lighting devices with respect to traffic
    control devices and adjacent residential districts;
    .4-2 (6).
         In residential building groups providing for permanent family occupancy,
    adequate amount and safe location of play areas for children and othe recrea-
    tional areas according to the concentration of occupancy;
    .4-2 (7).
          In business building groups near or abutting residential districts, fences,
    walls or year-round screen planting when necessary to shield adjacent residential
    districts from parking lot illumination, headlights, fumes, heat, blowing papers

           ~4-3 (I). R-2 AND R-3 DISTRICTS.
                The following required spacing between buildings shall be measured per-
           pendicularly from any exterior wall exposing windows or an entrance way; it
           does not apply in corner-to-comer placement of buildings where perpendicular
           wall exposures do not overlap;
                .4-3 (1) (a) . In buildings containing multiple dwelling units, walls con-
           taining main window exposures or main entrances shall be so oriented as to in-
           sure adequate light and air exposure; shall be so arranged as to avoid undue ex-
           posure to nearby through traffic ways or undue exposure to concentrated loading
           or parking facilities; shall be so oriented as to preserve visual and audible privacy
           as between adjacent buildings;
               .4-3 (I) (b). A building wall exposing both windows and an entrance
           way shall be located no closer to another building than a distance equal to the
           height of the taller building of the two, but in no case less than 50 feet;
               .4-3 (I) ( c). A building wall exposing only windows or only an entrance
           way shall be located no closer to another building than a distance equal to the
           height of the taller building of the two; but in no case less than 25 feet;
                .4-3 (I) ( d). Any open court area which otherwise complies with stand-
           ards of minimum spacing and open area of window exposure must, in any case,
           leave at least 25 per cent of its perimeter free and unobstructed for access by
           emergency vehicles;
               .4-3 (I) ( e) . A building group may not be so arranged that any tem-
           porarily or permanently inhabited building is inaccessible by emergency vehicles.

                    CONTINUANCE OF NON-CONFORMING STRUCTURES.
                Subject to all limitations herein set forth, any non-conforming structure
           may he occupied and operated and maintained' in a stale of good repair, hut
           no non-conforming structure shall he enlarged or extended unless the en-
           largement or extension can --be, and is,_ made in compliance with all of the
           provisions of this ordin110ce established for structures in the- district in which
           the non-conforming structure is located; •provided, however:
           .2-2 (1).
                A detached garage which is a non-conforming structure solely because
           such detached garage , is not set in from the side line of the Zon_e Lot at least
           the distance required by this ordinance, may he enlarged and extended, subject
           to the limitations established for Accessory Uses in the distrid in which the
           garage is located, so. long as ~no part of- such detached garage, after enlarge-
           ment and extension, is more than thirty feet from the reat line of the Zone
           Lot on which the detached garage is located. -

           .2-3.    TERMINATION OF NON-CONFQRMING STRUCTURES.
           .2-3 ( 1) • ACCIDENTAL DAMAGE TO STRUCTURE. ,
              - The ' right to operate and maintain any non-conforming structure shall
           terminate and shall cease to -exist whenever the non-conforming structure is
           accidentally damaged in any manrier and from any cause whatsoever and the
           cost of repairing such accidental damage exceeds seventy-five ~ cent of
                                                                                                 J
           the replacement cost of such structure on the da!e of such accidental damage.

           .2-3 (2). OBSOLESCENCE OF STRUCTURE.
              - The right to operate and maintafo _a ny non-cooforming structure shall
           terminate and shall cease to exist whenever the non-confoI1!)lllg structure be-
           comes obsolete or sub-standard under any applicable ordinance of the munici-
           pality and th~ cost of placing such structure in lawful compliance with. the
           applicable ordinaoce _exceeds fifty per cent -of the replacement cost of such
           structure on the date that the proper official of the municipality determines
           that such structurC" is obsolete or sub-standard .

           .2-3 (3).       DETERMINATION OF REPLACEMENT CosT.

                In determining µie replacement I.lost of any non-conforming structure there
           shall not be included therein the cost of land or any fact6rs other than the
           non-conforming structure itself.

                                           618.
                       Amendment Procedure
     618.1.       DECLARATION OF PUBLIC POLITY.

        .2.       LIMITATIONS ON ALL PROPOSED AMENDMENTS .
        .2-1.       Administrative Examination.
        .2-2.       Uniformity of District Regulations and Restrictions.

        .3-2.       Filing of Applications•
        .3-3.       Contents of Applications.
        .4.       EXAMINATION OF APPLICATIONS .
        .4-1.       Administrative Examination.
        .5.       DISPOSITION OF APPLICATIONS .
        .5-1.       Administrative Disposition.
        .5-2.       Legislative Disposition.

    618.1.      Declaration of Public Policy.
          For the purpose of establishing and maintaining sound, stable and desirable
    development within the territorial limits of the municipality, this ordinance,
    and as here used the term ordinance shall be deemed to include the official map,
    shall not be amended except to correct a manifest error in the ordinance or,
    because of changed or changing conditions in a particular area or in the
    municipality generally, to re-zone an area or extend the boundary of an exist-
    ing district, or to change the regulations and restrictions thereof, only as reason-
    ably necessary to the promotion of the public health, safety or general welfare.
    Subject to the limitations of the foregoing Declaration of Public Policy, an
    amendment to this ordinance may be initiated by Council on its own motion or,
    in the manner and pursuant to the procedure hereinafter set forth, may be
    initiated by any person, firm or corporation filing an application therefor with
    the Department of Zoning Administration.

    618.2.      liff!itations on all Proposed Amendments.
         All proposed amendments to this ordinance, regardless of how or by whom
    initiated, shall he suhjecf to the following limitations:
