                                                                                                )
           .2-1. ADMINISTRATIVE EXAMINATION.
               No amendment to this ordinance shall be adopted until the amendment
           has been examined by the administrative agencies as hereinafter set forth and
           the recommendations of those agencies obtained .

           .2-2.   UNIFORMITY OF DISTRICT REGULATIONS AND RESTRICTIONS.
                 No amendment to this ordinance shall be adopted whereby the ngalations
           and restrictions established thereby are not uniform for each district having
           the same classification and bearing the same symbol or designation on the of-
           ficial map, nor shall any amendment to this ordinance be adopted whereby dis-
           tricts having the same classification and bearing the same symbol or designation
           on the official map are subject to different regulations and restrictions.
           .2-3.   MINIMUM SIZE OF NEW DISTRICTS.
                No amendment to this ordinance shall be adopted whereby the zoning
           classification of an area is changed unless the area meets the following require-
           ments ·as to minimum size:
           .2-3 (1).
                A proposed R-O or R-1 District shall be large enough in gross area so
           that at least one-half of the number of existing building sites included in
           the proposed district are entirely within the interior of the district and do
           not abut a Business or Industrial District or an R-2, R-3 or R-4 District. For
           the purposes of this section, all vacant land included in the proposed district
           shall be divided into minimum size Zone Lots permitted in an R-O or R-1 District
           for structures designed or used, either wholly or partly, for residential occu-
           pancy;
           .2-3 (2).
                A proposed B-1 or B-2 District shall contain at least 70,000 square feet
           of gross area; provided, however, that a proposed B-1 District which would
           abut an existing B-2, B-3, B-4, B-5, 1-1 or 1-2 District shall be exempt from any
           requirement of minimum size;
           .2-3 (3).
                A proposed R-2, R-3, R-4, B-3, B-4, B-5, 1--0, 1-1 or 1-2 District shall
           contain at least eight acres of gross area; provided, however, that a proposed
           1-0 District which would abut an existing 1-1 or 1-2 District shall be exempt
           from any requirement of minimum size.
           .2-4.  ABUTMENT OF INCOMPATIBLE DISTRICTS.
                No amendment to this ordinance shall be adopted whereby any boundary
           line of an R-3, R-4, B-5, 1-1 or 1-2 District would abut directly upon the boun-
           dary line of an existing R-0, R-1 or R-2 District unless the boundary line is
           coincidental with the center line of a street, alley or other public property of
           comparable width.
           .2-5. ABUTMENT OF COMPATIBLE DISTRIGTS.
                No amendment to this ordinance shall be adopted whereby a P-1 District
           zoning classification is established for an area unless the area abuts an existing
           R-4, B-1, B-2, B-3, B-4, B-5, 1-1 or 1-2 District.
           •2-6. NEED FOR USES TO BE CLEAR AND DEMONSTRABLE.
                No amendment to this ordinance shall be adopted whereby a B-4 District
           zoning classification is established for an area unless there is a clear and

=== page 195 ===
                                                                           53        225




demonstrable necessity in the area for tboee uses which are permitted in a
B-4 District and are not permitted in a B-3 District.
.2-7.   PUBLIC HEARING REQUIRED. NOTICE GIVEN.
      No amendment to this ordinance shall become effective until after a public
hearing in relation thereto at which hearing parties in interest and cituens
shall have an oppotlunity to he heard. Notice of the time and place of such
hearing shall he published at least once in the official newspaper at least
fifteen days prior to the hearing. Additionally, any area for which an indi•
vidual application for a change in zoning classification is being considered,
shall he posted for at least fifteen days prior to the hearing. The posted notices
shall he in number, size and location as prescribed by the Department of Zoning
Administration and shall indicate the present zoning classification, the pro-
posed zoning classification, the time and place of the public hearing and any
other information prescribed by the Department.
.2-8.  EFFECT OF PROTEST TO AMENDMENT.
     In case of a protest to a proposed amendment to this ordinance, signed by
the owners of twenty per cent or more, either of the. area of the lots included
in such proposed change or of those immediately adjacent in the rear thereof~
extending one hundred feet therefrom, or of those directly opposite theretQ,
extending one hundred feet from the street frontage of such opposite lots,
such amendment shall not become effective except by the favorable vote . of
three-fourths of all members of Council.


618.3. Applications for Amendments .
•3-1. BY WHOM MADE.
     Any person, firm or corporation may apply for an amendment to this
ordinance•
.3-2.  FILING OF APPLICATIONS.
     All applications for amendments to this ordinance shall he filed in writing
with the Department of-'Zoning Administration.     ·               ·
.3-3 . . CONTENTS OF APPLICATIONS.
   . Without in any way limiting the right to file additional material, all appli~
cations for amendments to this ordinance shall contain, at least, the following:
.3-3 (1).
     The applicant's name, address and interest in the application and the
name, address and interest of every person, firm or corporation represented
by the applicant in the application; provided, however, that if the application
is for the designation of an area as a B-2 or B-3 District it shaB contain
the concurrence of the owner or owners of the entire land area to he included
within the proposed district, the owner or owners of all structures then existing
thereon and all incurnhrancers of said land area and structures and, additionally,
shall contain sufficient evidence to establish that the applicants ·are all the
owners and incumbrancers of the designated land area and structure~ intend
actually to develop the designated area and have both the means and ability
so to do;
.3-3 (2).
     The nature, description and effect of the proposed amendment;

               All other circumstances, factors and reasons which applicant offers in

    618.4.   Examination of Applications.
    .41.   ADMINISTRATIVE EXAMINATION.
         Upon receipt of an application for an amendment, properly and completely
    made as hereinahove set forth, the Department of Zoning Administration ehall
    transmit copies of the application to the Planning Oflice and to any other ~ .
    either public or private, which might be affected Ly the amendment. If con-
    sidered nece5Sllry, any such agency may require the applicant to furnish addi-
    tional information of a pertinent and reasonable nature. Any such agency
    may transmit comments and recommendations concerning the application to
    the Department of Zoning Administration.

    618.5.   Disposition of Applications .
    .5-1.  ADMINISTRATIVE EXAMINATION.
         When an application for an amendment, including agency comments and
    recommendations thereon, is completely assembled, the Department of Zoning
    Administration shall submit to Council such completed application and agency
    reports.
    .5-2. LEGISLATIVE DISPOSITION.
         Council shall examine all applications and agency reports submitted to it
    and shall direct the Department of Zoning Administration as to further action
    thereon.

                .41. Laboratory, Medical or Dental: a laboratory which provides bac-
           teriological, biological, medical, x-ray, pathological and similar analytical or

           shall be the center line of the part so selectee.

           two lot lines intersecting it at right angles or within 45 degrees of a right angle.

           more rooms used for transient occupancy.

     .46. Multiple Unit Dwelling: a single detached structure structurally

     47. Museum: a non-profit, non-commercial establishment operated as a
repository or a collection of nature, sdentific or literary curiosities or objects
of interest or works of art, not including the regular sale or distribution of the
objects collected.
     .48. Outdoor Commercial Advertising Device: a visible, immobile con•
trivance or structure in any shape or form, the purpose of which is to advertise
any product or service, campaign, event, etc.
     .49. Parish house: a residence for a minister, priest or rabbi in con-
nection with the operation of a church.
     .50. Permanent Occupancy: the rental of housing accommodations or
rooms on a week to week, month to month or year to year basis with a fixed
rent for each period of occupancy.
     .51. Permitted Structure: a structure meeting all the requirements es-
tablished by this ordinance for the district in which the structure is located.
    .52. Permitted Use: any use listed as a Use by Right, a Use           hJTem-
porary Permit, a home occupation or an accessory use in any given district.
    .53. Pole Sign: any sign attached to a pole supported by the ground
on a pavement or any low structure•
     .54. Premises: a general term meaning part or all of any Zone Lot or
part or all of any building or structure or group of buildings or structures
located thereon.
      •55. Pre-school: day care and education of five or more children aged
5 years and under.

terials such as the chemical composition or physical qualities. Does not in-
clude operations described as fabrication .
    .57. Project Sign: any sign established, maintained or applying to two
or more establishments or uses.
